# VedicHour — Relaunch Product & Architecture Spec

_Synthesised from 6 parallel research agents (quiz→paywall mechanics, founder monetization playbooks, Vedic competitor pricing, **India subscription-payment law**, non-AI design language, daily retention loops). Every price and benchmark below is evidence-backed; sources listed at the end._

**Status: authoritative.** Where this contradicts an earlier assumption, this wins.

---

## 1. Business model

## VERDICT ON THE OWNER'S ASSUMPTIONS — READ THIS FIRST

**₹1,999/month is wrong and it will kill the relaunch on its own.** Three independent research tracks converge: Sri Mandir (40M downloads, closest possible comparable) earns ₹600–800 per Indian user **per year**. AstroTalk's estimated LTV is ~₹1,500 **lifetime**. Indian recurring-subscription anchors sit at ₹129–299/mo (YouTube ₹129, Spotify ₹139–299, Hotstar ₹299). Nebula's annual is $39.99 (~₹3,400). ₹1,999/mo = ₹24,000/yr is ~6x Nebula into the worst-converting subscription region on earth (RevenueCat: India+SEA trial-to-paid 15.2%, D35 download-to-paid 1.37%). Your own codebase already prices `monthly` at ₹1,499 **one-time** (`src/lib/ziina/server.ts` line 36) — the pivot as specified is a 16x price increase on a product with zero paying customers.

**The number was right. The denominator was wrong.** ₹1,999 is a perfect *annual* price: it reads as "less than two astrologer calls" against AstroTalk's own published ₹500–1,500 per session.

**The card-required free trial is also wrong — but the instinct behind it is right.** India has 111.2M credit cards against 1,005.2B debit cards (<8% coverage). When RBI's card e-mandate rules landed, ~70% of recurring card payments failed in the first month. Worse, RBI *mandates* that the user's own bank send a pre-debit alert 24h before every auto-debit with one-tap cancel — the "forgot to cancel" revenue every Western case study quietly depends on is **legislated out of existence in India**. A card-gated free trial converts near zero here regardless of how good the quiz is.

**The 60% paywall-conversion target is not reachable and planning against it will misprice everything by 3–4x.** Split it into two instrumented numbers: quiz-complete → paid pass (target 25–35%) and pass → subscription (target 35–45%).

---

## THE OFFERING — TWO PRODUCTS, ONE SUBSCRIPTION, NO FEATURE MATRIX

**VedicHour** = one subscription that unlocks everything. Plan cards differentiate on **duration only**, never features (this is what Cal AI, Fastic, Fabulous and Quitzilla all do).

**Product 1 — "Aapka Samay" (Your Timing).** The daily personalised muhurat: today's 2–3 favourable windows and 1 to avoid, computed from *this user's* natal chart × running Mahadasha/Antardasha × today's transits × their city's panchang. Plus the 90-day dated window calendar, the current dasha in plain language with real dates, the evening check-in and the weekly alignment score. **This is the daily habit and the retention engine.**

**Product 2 — "Kundli Milan" (Partner Compatibility).** Ashtakoot 36-point Guna Milan broken down koot by koot, Mangal Dosha with cancellation rules stated explicitly, a plain-language "what will actually be hard between you two", and — the differentiator nobody ships — **best marriage windows in the next 24 months from both charts**. `/api/synastry` already computes the core of this.

Kundli Milan is **included** in the subscription, not sold separately. It is collected inside the quiz (Q2 branch) so the recap can show a blurred, specific compatibility card before the paywall. Including it raises perceived value of ₹1,999 far more than it costs.

**Do NOT build:** an AI-astrologer chatbot as the hero (AstroSage already ships a *talking* AI astrologer in every Indian language with a free minute; InstaAstro raised $12M to do the same — the moat is gone). A gemstone/puja store (it is the exact upsell users complain about in every competitor review). A live-astrologer marketplace (needs supply you don't have and re-introduces the meter you're positioning against).

---

## PRICE LADDER — INDIA (all prices GST-inclusive, displayed in ₹, no USD anywhere)

| SKU | Price | Billing | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **VedicHour Pass** | **₹99** | one-time, UPI intent, **no mandate**, 14 days full access | This *is* the trial |
| **VedicHour Year** | **₹1,999/yr** | UPI Autopay mandate | **Default. Pre-selected. "Most chosen."** Shown as "₹166/month — less than one 10-minute astrologer call." Strike-through anchor: ~~₹4,788 if paid monthly~~ |
| **VedicHour Monthly** | **₹399/mo** | UPI Autopay mandate | Deliberately unattractive third card. Exists to make annual look correct and to catch commitment-refusers |
| **Marriage Decision Plan** | **₹2,999 / 3 months** | one-time | Surfaced **only** when the quiz flags a marriage decision (Q1/Q7). Includes one human-reviewed kundli milan. This is the *only* place four-figure pricing is defensible — Shaadi.com Gold Plus runs ≈₹1,090/mo against exactly this intent |
| **Single Dated Answer** | **₹199** | one-time | Downsell rung 3. Dynamically titled from their Q10 event: "Best dates for your interview on 12 November." Monetizes the 85–95% who will never subscribe, and matches how India already buys astrology |

**No weekly plan.** India/SEA prefers weekly (36% of subs, highest globally) and weekly retains **3.4% at Year 1** vs 44.1% for annual. The ₹99 Pass satisfies the short-commitment preference without destroying LTV.

## PRICE LADDER — DIASPORA (US/UK/UAE/CA/AU/SG/NZ, IP + Accept-Language detected)

$4.99 Pass (14 days) · **$59.99/yr (default)** · $9.99/mo. Sri Mandir earns ~₹7,000 (~$81) ARPU abroad vs ₹600–800 in India — **~10x**, from ~2.6% of its users producing ~20% of revenue. This is where the ₹1,999/mo *ambition* actually lives. Your traffic is already geo-segmented at the door; running one global price throws away a documented 10x spread at zero product cost.

Every price is a **row in a `pricing_config` Supabase table read at request time** — never hardcoded. `ZIINA_PLANS` must stop being the source of truth. Puff Count A/B-tested $4 to $12 without shipping a build; you cannot discover the right Indian astrology price if it's compiled in.

---

## TRIAL MECHANIC — ₹99 PAID 14-DAY PASS, NO MANDATE

This is the single most important decision in the document.

**How it works:** at the end of the quiz, ₹99 via **UPI intent** (PhonePe/GPay/Paytm one-tap, no card, no saved instrument, no mandate, no auto-renew) buys 14 days of everything. On day 11, WhatsApp + in-app: *"Your Pass ends in 3 days. Continue for a year at ₹1,999 — we will never charge you without telling you 24 hours first."* Only at that upgrade do you register a **UPI Autopay mandate** via Razorpay.

**Why this beats both a free trial and a card-required trial:**

1. **It honours the owner's actual instinct.** Money changes hands before value is delivered. You capture most of the opt-out-trial advantage (48.8% vs 18.2%) because these users have already paid — while sidestepping every India-specific failure mode.
2. **It dodges the RBI pre-debit alert entirely during the evaluation window.** No mandate exists, so no bank sends a cancel prompt on day 6. That single mechanic is what breaks the Western playbook here.
3. **It dodges the ~8% credit-card problem.** UPI intent is the payment method 30–60-year-old Tier-2/3 Indians actually use, and it has no mandate registration friction.
4. **It is the anti-complaint.** Every incumbent is destroyed in reviews for exactly this: InstaAstro's "₹1 trial chat" not honoured with wallet auto-deducted; AstroTalk's unexpected charges after clicking a trial offer; refunds paid as app credit not cash. "₹99. That's the whole charge. Nothing renews unless you choose it" is a claim no incumbent can copy without destroying 90% of their revenue.
5. **14 days, not 7 or 3.** RevenueCat: 17–32 day trials convert at 42.5% vs 25.5% for ≤4 days. 55.4% of 3-day-trial cancellations happen on Day 0. An astrology product's value is *falsifiable over time* — a user has to live through days you flagged. 14 days gives them two full weekends and at least one dated event.

**Experiment #1 after launch is ₹49 vs ₹99 vs ₹149 on the Pass.** ₹99 is the opening bet, not a conviction.

---

## PROCESSOR

**Razorpay for INR.** Non-negotiable. You already have `supabase/migrations/20260406_razorpay_payments.sql` (amounts in paise) — the integration was started and abandoned for Ziina. Reinstate it.
- ₹99 Pass → Razorpay Orders API, UPI intent, no mandate.
- ₹1,999/yr and ₹399/mo → **Razorpay Subscriptions with UPI Autopay**, ₹1 authorisation to register the mandate.
- Both amounts sit far under the **₹15,000 AFA-free cap**, so renewals need no OTP.
- Cap the mandate max at **₹4,999** so you never brush the cap or the UPI Autopay ceiling.
- Cards and netbanking as secondary options, listed **below** UPI.

**Stripe for USD/GBP/AUD diaspora.** Switch on the same `x-currency` header the `/upsell` route already reads via `currencyFromHeader`.

**Ziina:** freeze it. Keep for AED only if it already works; do not extend it. It solves nothing for Indian recurring billing.

---

## LEGAL / ENTITY PREREQUISITES — START THESE ON DAY 1, THEY ARE CALENDAR TIME NOT BUILD TIME

1. **Indian entity + current account.** Razorpay Subscriptions and UPI Autopay require a registered Indian entity (Pvt Ltd, LLP, or registered proprietorship) with PAN, an Indian current account, and KYC. If this does not exist, **nothing else on this list matters** — it is the critical path.
2. **Astrology-category underwriting approval — get this in writing before you build.** Astrology/occult services sit in the restricted or enhanced-scrutiny category for most Indian payment aggregators. Approval for recurring mandates in this category can take weeks and can be refused. **Email your Razorpay account manager on day one with your exact use case (subscription, UPI Autopay, astrology guidance, no per-minute consultation) and get a written yes before a single line of billing code is written.** Have Cashfree as the named fallback.
3. **GST.** 18% on B2C digital services. ₹1,999 inclusive nets ~₹1,694. Display GST-inclusive prices only. Register before first ₹.
4. **Export of services for the diaspora tier.** Billing US/UK customers from an Indian entity is a zero-rated export requiring an LUT (or paying IGST and claiming refund) plus FIRC handling. **Decide before build** whether the USD tier runs through the Indian entity under LUT or through a separate US/UAE entity — this changes the processor, the invoicing, and the accounting.
5. **RBI e-mandate compliance as product, not checkbox.** 24h pre-debit notification, self-serve pause/modify/cancel, mandate visible to the user. Build the notification as a *feature* you send yourself 36h before the bank sends theirs.
6. **Content policy with hard exclusions, built into the generation prompt.** No medical-deferral advice ("do not have the procedure today"), no specific investment instruction, no litigation-outcome prediction. This is a real-harm surface, not a disclaimer problem.
7. **Refund policy legal review.** The "if a window we gave you was wrong" guarantee language needs a lawyer's eyes before it ships, and refunds must be paid in **cash, not credit** — that is the differentiator.

## 2. Onboarding flow (quiz → paywall)

## PRINCIPLE: 15 QUESTIONS, 31 SCREENS, ~7 MINUTES

The owner's "12–15 questions" is the right number of *questions* and the wrong number of *screens*. Noom runs ~113 screens; Fastic 68 (5:55); Yazio 60+; Cal AI 32. Lose It! told RevenueCat trial starts "went up double digits as onboarding got longer." What makes a long flow feel short is **screens that ask nothing** — reassurance, payoff, social proof, education, loaders. Interleave one every 2–3 questions.

**Route:** `/start` (replace the 5-line redirect currently at `src/app/(app)/onboarding/page.tsx`). Progress bar visible from screen 1, labelled in steps not percentages. Back arrow always live. Every answer persists to `localStorage` + a server `quiz_sessions` row so a dropped session resumes. No keyboard required except S5, Q13, Q14.

---

### S0 — LANGUAGE (before any question)

> **आप किस भाषा में पढ़ना चाहेंगे?**
> Which language should we use?

Large tap cards: `English` · `हिंदी` · `Hinglish` · `मराठी` · `বাংলা` · `తెలుగు` · `தமிழ்`
No skip. Sets `locale` on every downstream screen, the paywall, the emails, the WhatsApp messages and the daily push.

*Why screen 0:* localisation is the highest-win-rate experiment category in the entire dataset (Adapty, **62.3% LTV win rate** vs 34.6% for visual redesigns). HDFC got 2x conversions at 40% lower CPA on Hindi creatives. It costs one tap and signals respect before you ask for anything.

---

### Q1 — INTENT (easy, single select, 6 icon cards)

> **What do you most want clarity on right now?**
> `Career or job change` · `Rishta / marriage` · `Money` · `Health — mine or family's` · `Property or a big purchase` · `Something feels stuck and I can't name it`

**BRANCH:** sets `primary_domain`, which selects the Q7 question set, the recap headline, the three locked cards, and whether the Marriage Decision Plan appears on the paywall.

*Indian intent, not Western personality framing. Kundali/birth chart is 65% of Indian astrology demand, marriage matching 52%; 60% of AstroTalk's revenue is marriage-related. "Know yourself" is a US product.*

---

### Q2 — SCOPE

> **Are you asking only for yourself?**
> `Just me` · `Me and my partner` · `Me and my child` · `Me and my whole family`

**BRANCH:** any non-"just me" sets `partner_module = true`, which inserts a second birth-details block after Q5 and turns Kundli Milan into an *included* feature on the paywall rather than an upsell.

---

### Q3 — BIRTH DATE

> **When were you born?**
Native date wheel, no keyboard, defaults to 1980.
Microcopy: *"Your date alone fixes your Moon sign. Time and place sharpen it."*

---

### Q4 — BIRTH TIME (highest drop-off risk in any Vedic funnel — design it as a feature, not a failure)

> **What time were you born?**
Time wheel, with **"I don't know" rendered as a visually equal third option**, never a small grey link.

**BRANCH on "I don't know" → Q4b:**
> **Roughly what part of the day?**
> `Before sunrise` · `Morning` · `Afternoon` · `Evening` · `Night` · `Truly no idea`

→ **S4c FULL-SCREEN REASSURANCE:**
> *"That's completely normal — most people don't know. Astrologers rectify birth times every day. We'll work from a rectified time and mark clearly anything that depends on it."*

This must **never** read as "your result will be worse." This is the largest recoverable cohort in the funnel and it has no analogue in the diet apps this playbook comes from.

---

### Q5 — BIRTH PLACE

> **Which city were you born in?**
City autocomplete, geolocation-biased, must include Tier-II/III towns. `/api/geocode` already handles this.

*(If `partner_module`, Q5b–Q5d repeat date/time/place for the partner, prefaced by: "Now theirs — this is what makes a real milan possible.")*

---

### S6 — FREE PAYOFF (no question — this is where the flow stops being an interrogation)

Full screen, chart wheel drawn in:

> ## You are a **Vrishchika** Moon.
> ### **Anuradha** nakshatra, pada 2.
>
> [one true, specific, chart-derived line]
>
> *"Most horoscope apps read your Sun sign. Vedic reads your Moon — that is why their predictions never quite fit."*

*Cheap for you, unavailable from any competitor's sun-sign horoscope, verifiably about them. This is the exchange that buys the next nine questions.*

---

### S7 — SOCIAL PROOF

A **precise, unrounded, real** number from your own DB — `1,84,203 charts calculated` (Indian digit grouping, never `184,203`). Three one-line testimonials in the selected language with first name, city, age. Precision is the device; Noom uses "3,627,436."

---

### Q6 — PAIN, GENTLE ENTRY

> **Honestly — how have the last six months gone?**
> `Much harder than usual` · `Up and down` · `Steady` · `Better than usual` · `I can't tell any more`

**BRANCH on "Much harder than usual" → reassurance interstitial:**
> *"That pattern shows up again and again in charts running a hard Mahadasha. Let's find out where yours is."*

---

### Q7 — PAIN, DOMAIN-SPECIFIC (branched from Q1)

> **Which is closest to your situation?**

**Career:** Stuck in the same role too long · Deciding whether to change jobs · Starting or running a business · Money comes but never stays · Waiting on someone else's decision
**Rishta/Marriage:** Looking for the right match · Match found but family isn't agreeing · Married, and there's tension · Long delay and I don't know why · Considering separation
**Money:** Income is fine but nothing saves · Debt I can't clear · A big investment decision · Family money disputes · Business cash flow
**Health:** My own health · A parent's health · Something recurring nobody can explain · Sleep and energy · Mental peace
**Property:** Buying · Selling · A dispute · Griha pravesh timing · Ancestral property
**Stuck:** shows all five domains as multi-select, max 2

---

### Q8 — TIMING ARCHETYPE (the Noom slider quiz, compressed to one question)

> **When things go wrong for you, what usually happens first?**
> `I act at the wrong moment` · `I wait too long and miss it` · `I get pulled into other people's problems` · `My health gives way` · `Money leaks out`

Produces a **named archetype** shown on the recap ("The one who waits too long"). Costs nothing; enormously personal.

---

### Q9 — PRIOR EXPERIENCE (this is where you earn the wedge)

> **Have you had your kundali read before?**
> `Yes, by a family astrologer` · `Yes, on an app or a paid call` · `No, first time` · `Many times — and I got a different answer every time`

**BRANCH on the last option → interstitial:**
> *"That's the complaint we hear most. We show you the calculation behind every prediction — the dasha, the transit, and the exact rule. You can check our work."*

Earn this here. Do not claim it on the landing page.

---

### Q10–Q11 — **THE VERIFIABLE PAST HIT** (the single highest-conviction screen in this document)

Compute the user's Vimshottari dasha sequence live (`ephemeris-service/main.py:calculate_vimshottari_dasha` already does this) and find the most recent Mahadasha/Antardasha change.

**Q10:**
> **Did something significant change for you around [computed year, e.g. 2019]?**
> `Yes — a big change` · `Yes, but small` · `Nothing I remember` · `Actually it was 2018 / 2020` (adjacent-year picker)

**Q11 (branched on any yes):**
> **What changed?**
> `Job or career` · `Marriage or relationship` · `Health` · `Moved city` · `Money — up or down` · `A loss in the family`

→ **S11b, FULL SCREEN, the proof:**
> ### In [2019] your **Shukra Mahadasha** ended and **Surya** began.
> That transition moves the centre of gravity of a life. It is why [their Q11 answer] happened when it did — and not two years earlier or later.
>
> *This is not a horoscope. It is arithmetic on your birth moment.*

**This screen is worth more than the other thirty combined.** It converts scepticism into belief with something checkable, personal, and impossible for a sun-sign app to fake. Every teardown in the research puts a "value moment" here; none of them can offer one this strong.

---

### Q12 — THE DATED EVENT (the highest-leverage question in the genre)

> **Is there a date coming up you're worried about — or hoping for?**
> `Job change or interview` · `Wedding or engagement` · `Property or a big purchase` · `Court case or dispute` · `Medical procedure` · `Exam or result` · `Travel or relocation` · `Nothing specific right now`

**Q12b if specific:** date picker — *"When is it?"*
**If "nothing specific":** *"What are the next 90 days mainly about?"* → anchor the chart on today+90.

*Vedic timing is literally about dates. "Your interview is on 12 November — here are the three windows we'd use" is more concrete and more falsifiable than anything a weight-loss app can promise. This date becomes the flag on the projection chart and the title of downsell rung 3.*

---

### Q13 — DELIVERY TIME (notification permission, reframed as plan configuration)

> **When should your timing for the day reach you?**
> `5–7am (Brahma muhurta)` · `7–9am` · `With lunch` · `Evening` · `The night before, for tomorrow`

Trigger the OS/browser permission prompt **immediately after this answer**, never before it.

---

### Q14 — THEIR OWN WORDS

> **In your own words — what should be different 90 days from now?**
Free text, optional, three tappable example chips in the chosen language.

Whatever they type becomes the **headline of the recap** and the first line of their day-1 card. One field does more for perceived personalisation than five multiple-choice questions.

---

### Q15 — MICRO-COMMITMENT (hold-to-sign)

> **Will you check your timing before any big decision for the next 14 days?**
> **[ Hold to commit ]** — a press-and-hold button that fills over 1.5 seconds. Not a tap.

Fabulous uses exactly this gesture. The physical effort is the point.

---

### S16 — EMAIL + WHATSAPP GATE (framed as delivery, never as signup)

> **Where should we send your timing profile?**
> [ email ]
> [ Also send it on WhatsApp (optional) ] — phone

CTA: **"Show me my profile"** — never "Sign up," never "Submit."

WhatsApp will outperform email for this audience and is your day-11 upgrade channel. `src/lib/notify/whatsapp.ts` already exists.

---

### S17 — THE LOADER (10–14 seconds, real computation, 2 questions interleaved)

Full screen, dark, chart wheel drawing itself. Four lines, each ticking green with a **real value**:

1. `Calculating planetary positions — Lahiri ayanamsa…` → **Done · 9 grahas placed**
2. `Finding your Mahadasha and Antardasha…` → **Shani → Budh**
3. `Checking transits against your 4th, 7th and 10th houses…` → **3 active**
4. `Building your 90-day timing calendar…` → **1,412 hours scored**

At ~4s, a question card interrupts over the loader:
> *"While we finish — do you keep any vrat or fasting days?"* `Yes, regularly` · `Sometimes` · `No` · `I'd like to start`

At ~9s:
> *"Should we include remedies — mantra, daan, gemstone — or only timing?"* `Both` · `Only timing` · `Only remedies`

Never exceed 15s. **Never a generic spinner.** Noom calls this "a fake loader that actually does real work" — for you the work is genuinely real, so the sunk-cost effect and the honesty coexist.

---

## S18 — THE RECAP: "Your Vedic timing profile is ready"

Scrollable, one block per screen-height, **CTA pinned to the bottom throughout**.

**BLOCK 1 — Identity.** `[Name] · Vrishchika Moon · Anuradha nakshatra, pada 2 · "The one who waits too long"`

**BLOCK 2 — Their words.** Their Q14 text quoted verbatim in quotation marks, with **"by [today + 90 days]"** beneath. Falls back to their Q7 answer if skipped.

**BLOCK 3 — Dasha card.** *"You are in Shani Mahadasha → Budh Antardasha until 14 March 2027."* One plain-language sentence in their language about what that period demands. No jargon without a gloss.

**BLOCK 4 — THE PROJECTION CHART.** The single asset the paywall sells. Build it properly; animate it drawing over 1.2s; make it screenshot-worthy.
- 90-day horizontal band, today at left, day 90 at right
- Their Q12b event as a **flagged marker labelled in their own words**: `Your interview · 12 Nov`
- 3–4 gold shaded **favourable windows** with dates and labels: `Strong window · 2–9 Nov`
- 1–2 terracotta **avoid windows**: `Avoid signing · 18–21 Nov`
- **Two lines**: flat dotted grey `acting at random` vs rising solid gold `acting on your windows`. This is Noom's steady-decline-vs-yo-yo device translated directly.

**BLOCK 5 — Three LOCKED cards, blurred, each labelled with a specific that hurts to leave.** Never a category noun.
- ✓ `Your best 3 days to sign the offer letter`
- ✓ `Why your marriage talks stalled in Aug–Sep`
- ✓ `Your Sade Sati: 2 years 4 months remaining`
- ✗ `Career insights` · ✗ `Relationship analysis`

**BLOCK 6 — One card genuinely UNLOCKED and free.** A real, checkable insight given away. This is what makes the locked five credible.

**BLOCK 7 — Combinatorial proof.** *"Built from your 15 answers, 9 planetary positions and 3 divisional charts — 1 of [N] possible profiles."* Compute N from your actual branch tree. Do not invent it.

**BLOCK 8 — CTA.** `Unlock my 90 days — ₹99` → straight to the paywall in the same session.

**Never email the recap and hope they come back.** Adapty: onboarding paywalls convert at 1.35% vs 0.89% in-app; Rootd 5x'd revenue moving the paywall earlier; ~50% of trial starts happen during onboarding.

---

## S19 — THE PAYWALL

One screen. No scroll at 375×812. Immediately after the recap, same session.

**HEADER**
> ## [Name], your 90 days are ready.
> **₹99 for 14 days. That is the whole charge — nothing renews unless you choose it.**

**THE HONESTY STRIP** (three dots on a line — this is Fabulous's trust architecture *and* your anti-competitor wedge):
`Today · ₹99, full access` → `Day 11 · we ask if you want to continue` → `Day 14 · access ends unless you say yes`

**THE CLAIM NOBODY ELSE CAN MAKE** — set above the price, large:
> ### ₹0 per minute. Koi wallet nahin. Koi surprise charge nahin.
> ✓ No per-minute charges — read for as long as you want
> ✓ We tell you 24 hours before any renewal
> ✓ Cancel in 2 taps. Refunds in cash, not credit.
>
> [screenshot of the actual cancel button]

*Engineered directly against the documented complaint set that destroys every incumbent: InstaAstro's unhonoured ₹1 trial with wallet auto-deduction, Astroyogi's balance draining through call drops, AstroTalk's month-long refunds paid as app credit.*

**PRIMARY CTA**
> **[ Start now — ₹99 ]**
> small below: *Pays once. Does not renew.*

**PAYMENT ROW — UPI FIRST**
`[UPI · PhonePe / GPay / Paytm]` then `[Card]` then `[Netbanking]`

**BELOW THE FOLD — the plans, for people who already know they want it**
> *Already know you want it? Skip the Pass:*
- **VedicHour Year — ₹1,999** · *₹166/month · ~~₹4,788 if paid monthly~~* · **Most chosen**
- VedicHour Monthly — ₹399

On every card the **billed total is the largest type**; the per-month figure is smaller and grey. Non-negotiable: Apple pulled Cal AI in April 2026 for showing the equivalent price more prominently than the billed amount, and RBI disclosure rules point the same way.

**MARRIAGE BRANCH ONLY:** if Q1/Q7 flagged a marriage decision, a fourth card appears — *Marriage Decision Plan · ₹2,999 for 3 months · includes one human-reviewed kundli milan.*

**TRUST ROW**
`Lahiri ayanamsa · Swiss Ephemeris · your birth data is never sold` + a **"See the calculation"** link that opens the actual planetary table. Nobody else shows their working. This is the wedge, rendered.

**SOCIAL PROOF:** one real unrounded number + three testimonials in their language with first name, city, age.

**NO countdown timer, no spin wheel, no mascot on paywall 1.** Cal AI's tested paywall has none of these. They belong on the downsell.

---

## DOWNSELL LADDER — 4 RUNGS, RUNG 3 IS THE MONEY

**Rung 1 (first dismiss)** — *"Start with just the question you came for."* Re-frame the ₹99 Pass around their Q1 intent. No discount yet.

**Rung 2 (second dismiss)** — *"Or take the year now."* ₹1,999 annual, shown once, no Pass. Catches the small cohort who won't buy a trial but will buy a product.

**Rung 3 (third dismiss) — THE ONE THING THEY CAME FOR.** Dynamically titled from Q12:
> ### Best dates for your interview on 12 November — ₹199, one time.

This monetizes the 85–95% who will never subscribe, and it matches how India already buys astrology: AstroTalk did ₹1,214 Cr in FY25 with 90–95% of revenue from per-consultation spend, not subscriptions.

**Rung 4 (exit)** — *"Send me my free Moon-sign summary instead"* (email already captured at S16) → 5-message win-back sequence on WhatsApp. Then let them leave. Do not prompt a fifth time.

---

## SEO EXPRESS PATH — DO NOT PUT A 15-QUESTION WALL IN FRONT OF SEARCH INTENT

You already have `/free-kundli`, `/moon-sign-calculator`, `/nakshatra-finder`, `/sade-sati-calculator`, `/vimshottari-dasha-calculator`, `/muhurat`, `/hora`, `/dasha` shipped. Every one of them gets a **3-question inline widget above the fold** — birth date, time (with "I don't know"), place — returning the S6 free Moon-sign payoff **in the page**. Only then: *"Want your next 90 days? Answer 12 more questions →"* which drops them into the full quiz at Q6.

Track SEO arrivals as a **separate cohort end to end**. Their pass-to-subscription rate will be materially lower than quiz-intent traffic and mixing the two corrupts every read you take.


---

## 3. Daily loop (the retention engine)

## THE STRUCTURAL ADVANTAGE NOBODY ELSE HAS

The panchang genuinely changes every single day, for every user, at zero marginal content cost. The daily return reason is **authentic, not manufactured gamification** — which is why this loop can be honest in a way Duolingo's streak cannot. Astrology apps report 30–40% DAU with daily horoscope driving ~68% of engagement. And the product is literally named after the mechanic: **muhurat**.

Existing muhurat tools (Drik Panchang, mPanchang, Choghadiya apps) serve **city-level** Choghadiya — identical for every user in Mumbai. **None overlays the user's own dasha and transits.** That is the entire product.

---

## THE HOME SCREEN — "Aaj ke shubh ghante" (Today's good hours)

The app opens here. Not a dashboard. Not a feed. One screen.

**1. The 24-hour band.** A horizontal bar of the day, now-marker sliding across it in real time. Gold = favourable, neutral = plain, terracotta = avoid. Rahu Kaal hatched. Sunrise and sunset marked. Tap any band to see *why*.

**2. Three lines, in their language, in this order:**
> **Best window today · 11:12am – 12:48pm**
> For the money conversation you flagged.
>
> **Avoid · 1:30pm – 3:00pm**
> Rahu Kaal over your 10th lord.
>
> **One thing to do today**
> Send the follow-up before noon. Don't wait for them to reply first.

**3. Category tags** matching how Indians actually use Choghadiya: `Paisa/Business` · `Yatra` · `Shaadi/Rishta` · `Naya kaam` · `Ghar` · `Sehat` · `Gaadi`.

**4. "See the calculation"** — always present, always one tap. Opens the actual planetary table, the running dasha, and the rule applied. This is the anti-AstroTalk move and it must never be buried.

**5. Below the fold:** their 90-day band with today highlighted, and their next flagged event with a countdown in days.

**The paywall line:** free/expired users get the generic **city-level** Choghadiya (matching what Drik Panchang gives away). Subscribers get the **personalised overlay**. Same screen, one blurred layer.

---

## MORNING NOTIFICATION — at their Q13-chosen time, in their S0 language

The rule: **a specific, dated, checkable instruction. Never a horoscope.**

✓ `Aaj 11:12–12:48 aapka sabse strong window hai. Salary ki baat abhi kariye.`
✓ `Today 11:12am–12:48pm is your strongest window. Ask for the number then.`
✗ `Today is a good day for communication.` ← if you ever ship this line, the product is dead

**Channel priority: WhatsApp first, web push second, email third.** For a 30–60 Indian mobile audience WhatsApp is the channel they actually read. Web push on Android Chrome works without install; **on iOS it requires the PWA be installed to the home screen** — which is exactly why the install moment below matters most for the diaspora tier.

**Event-triggered variants:**
- **3 days before their Q12 event:** `Aapka interview 3 din baad hai. Usse pehle 2 windows hain — 9 Nov aur 11 Nov.`
- **Day of a flagged avoid window:** sent the night before, not the morning of.
- **Dasha/antardasha change:** `Aaj se aapki Budh Antardasha shuru. Agle 2 saal 8 mahine ka rang badal raha hai.` — the one message no other app on their phone can send.

---

## EVENING CHECK-IN — 20 seconds, 3 taps

At 9pm local (offset from their morning slot):

> **Aaj kaisa gaya? Jaisa bataya tha?**
> `Behtar` (better) · `Waisa hi` (about right) · `Bhaari` (heavier)
> *+ optional one-line note*

**This already exists.** `supabase/migrations/20260702_day_ratings.sql` ships the table — `rating` as -1/0/+1, `predicted_score` snapshotted at rating time, unique per `(user_id, rated_date)` — and `/api/day-rating` is live. Wire the loop to it; do not rebuild it.

---

## STREAK — count the act, not the app

**Wrong:** days you opened the app.
**Right:** **"days you used your window"** — a day counts when the user either checks in that evening *or* taps a window before 6pm. That converts a gimmick into the product's actual use case.

Display as a small, calm number, not a flaming emoji. Milestones at 7, 21, 40 (mandala), 108. At 21 days: *"Teen hafte. Ab yeh aadat hai."*

Duolingo's own Streak Wager research shows statistically significant D1/D7/D14 lifts with D7 retention +14%. Day 0–7 is the habit-formation threshold — which is another reason the Pass runs 14 days, not 3.

---

## WEEKLY RECAP — Sunday evening, the most important surface in the product

> ## Aapka hafta
>
> **Aapke clearer windows 5 dinon mein 7 mein mile.**
> *Your clearer windows aligned on 5 of the 7 days you rated.*
>
> [7 dots, each gold/neutral/terracotta, each with the user's own check-in beside it]
>
> **Best day:** Wednesday — you rated it "better," we had scored it 84.
> **We got Friday wrong.** We scored it 71; you felt it heavy. Shukra was closer to your natal Mars than our threshold caught.
>
> **Next week:** two strong windows, Tuesday 10:40–12:10 and Saturday 7:15–9:00. One to avoid: Thursday afternoon.

Four jobs, one screen:
1. **Retention** — it is the reason to still be here next Sunday.
2. **Day-11 upgrade evidence** — this exact screen is the upgrade email. Not a discount. Evidence.
3. **Renewal justification** — twelve months of these is why the mandate stays alive.
4. **The only honest accuracy claim you will ever be able to make** — it is computed from *their* check-ins, about *their* days.

**Admitting the miss is the whole trick.** Every competitor claims perfection; none can show their working. Naming the day you got wrong is what makes the four you got right believable. It is also a moat — AstroTalk's per-minute model has no equivalent and cannot build one.

**Language rule (legal):** frame as *alignment* and *reflection*, never as "prediction accuracy proof." The existing `day_ratings` migration comment already sets this convention — keep it.

---

## MONTHLY — "What changes this month"

One card on the 1st, naming the specific transit and its dates. Ties the daily noise back to the dasha arc so the subscription feels like a story, not a lottery.

---

## PWA INSTALL MOMENT

**Primary: the confirmation screen of their FIRST evening check-in (day 1, ~9pm).** They have just experienced the loop end to end — a morning instruction, a lived day, a check-in. The prompt is not "install our app," it is:

> **Kal ka window subah 6:45 par bhejein?**
> *Add VedicHour to your home screen so tomorrow's window reaches you on time.*
> **[ Add to home screen ]** · *Not now*

`src/app/manifest.ts` already exists. What's missing is a service worker, VAPID keys, and the install-prompt capture.

**Secondary moment: day 3, on hitting the first streak milestone** — for users who dismissed the first prompt.

**Never on first page load.** Never as a banner over the recap or the paywall.

---

## TRIAL → SUBSCRIPTION SEQUENCE (the ₹99 Pass, 14 days)

| Day | Channel | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **0, +2h** | WhatsApp | *"Your first window is tomorrow, 11:12am–12:48pm. Here's what to use it for."* |
| **1, 9pm** | in-app | First check-in + PWA install prompt |
| **3** | WhatsApp | References their Q12 event **by name and date**: *"Aapka interview 9 din baad hai. Usse pehle 2 windows mark kiye hain."* |
| **7** | in-app | First weekly recap — their alignment score |
| **11** | WhatsApp + in-app | **THE UPGRADE MESSAGE.** Their second weekly recap as the body. *"Aapka Pass 3 din mein khatam. Saal bhar ke liye ₹1,999 — hum kabhi bina 24 ghante pehle bataye charge nahi karenge."* Evidence, not discount. |
| **13** | WhatsApp | *"Kal aakhri din. Ek tap mein continue kar sakte hain."* |
| **14** | in-app | Access ends. Home screen degrades to generic city Choghadiya with their own last alignment score visible above it. |
| **21** | WhatsApp | Win-back timed to **a real astrological event in their chart**, not a generic offer: *"Aaj se aapki Budh Antardasha shuru ho rahi hai."* The one message only you can send. |

---

## RENEWAL & DUNNING (once a mandate exists)

- **T-36h before any auto-debit:** your own message, before the bank's. *"Kal ₹1,999 renew hoga. Yeh saal aapke 5 of 7 din align hue. Rokna ho toh yahan — do tap."* You cannot prevent the bank's RBI-mandated alert at T-24h, so **own it first and convert the fear into trust**. Nobody in this category does this.
- **Failed debit:** retry days 1, 3, 5, 7, 10 — ~90% of recovered transactions land inside the first 10 days; Recurly reports 49% annual recovery. Message on **WhatsApp, not email**.
- **Cancel flow:** offer **pause (1 or 3 months)** and downgrade to monthly before the cancel button. Pause/skip beats binary stay-or-cancel. But the cancel button must be **genuinely two taps, self-serve, no chat, no retention wall** — you screenshotted it on the paywall; honour it.
- **Month-1 intervention (day 21 of an annual):** 35% of annual cancellations happen in month 1. Send a personal-feeling message showing their **next dasha change with a date** — a concrete reason to still be subscribed in month 2.


---

## 4. Architecture

## WHAT EXISTS (verified in `C:\Users\user\OneDrive\Documents\Projects\astrology\jyotish-ai`)

Next.js **14.2.35** App Router · `@supabase/ssr` + `supabase-js` · **Inngest 4.2.4** (background jobs) · **Upstash Redis + Ratelimit** · **PostHog** · framer-motion · `@react-pdf/renderer` · Anthropic + OpenAI + Gemini SDKs · Playwright + Vitest.

Python **FastAPI** ephemeris at `ephemeris-service/main.py` (Swiss Ephemeris, Lahiri, sidereal-mode reasserted per-request via middleware) exposing `/natal-chart`, `/panchang`, `/hora-schedule`, `/choghadiya`, `/rahu-kaal`, `/full-day-data`, with `calculate_vimshottari_dasha`, `get_current_dasha`, `classify_functional_nature`, `compute_hora_base_for_lagna`.

Already there and reusable: `supabase/migrations/20260406_razorpay_payments.sql` (paise, `razorpay_order_id`/`payment_id`, RLS) — **started then abandoned for Ziina**. `supabase/migrations/20260702_day_ratings.sql` (the evening check-in table, already built correctly). `src/lib/notify/whatsapp.ts`. `src/app/manifest.ts`. `/api/day-rating`, `/api/synastry`, `/api/kundali`, `/api/geocode`, `/api/track`, `/api/cron/*`. Seven SEO calculator routes already shipped.

**Missing entirely: any i18n. `grep -rl "i18n\|locale" src/lib` returns nothing.**

---

## THE CORE STRUCTURAL PROBLEM

Everything is **report-scoped and one-time**. `reports.payment_status` carries entitlement (`src/lib/reports/entitlement.ts` is explicit about this), `reports.day_scores` is a JSONB blob generated once, and `ZIINA_PLANS` in `src/lib/ziina/server.ts` is a compiled-in price table of one-time purchases. A recurring product needs entitlement that is **user-scoped, feature-scoped and time-bounded**, and day scores that are **generated forever, nightly, for every active subscriber**.

Do **not** try to make `reports` carry this. Add alongside; migrate reads.

---

## 1. DATA MODEL — NEW TABLES

```sql
-- Immutable chart, deduplicated across users by birth data
natal_charts (
  id uuid pk,
  chart_hash text unique,        -- sha256(birth_date, birth_time, lat, lng)
  birth_date date, birth_time text, birth_lat numeric, birth_lng numeric,
  tz_offset_minutes int,
  time_confidence text,          -- 'exact' | 'rectified' | 'sunrise'  ← Q4 branch
  positions jsonb,               -- 9 grahas, sidereal longitudes, houses
  lagna_sign text, moon_sign text, moon_nakshatra text, nakshatra_pada int,
  dasha_sequence jsonb,          -- FULL Vimshottari tree, computed ONCE
  functional_nature jsonb,       -- from classify_functional_nature()
  hora_base jsonb,
  computed_at timestamptz
)
-- chart_hash dedup is not a micro-optimisation: twins, families and
-- re-entrant quiz sessions collapse onto one row and one ephemeris call.

subscriptions (
  id uuid pk, user_id uuid,
  plan_code text,                -- 'pass_14d' | 'year_in' | 'month_in' | 'year_us' ...
  state text,                    -- trialing|active|past_due|paused|cancelled|expired
  processor text,                -- 'razorpay' | 'stripe'
  processor_sub_id text, processor_customer_id text,
  currency text, amount_minor int,
  current_period_start timestamptz, current_period_end timestamptz,
  cancel_at_period_end bool default false,
  paused_until date,
  started_at timestamptz, cancelled_at timestamptz, cancel_reason text
)

payment_mandates (                -- RBI e-mandate is a first-class object
  id uuid pk, user_id uuid, subscription_id uuid,
  rail text,                      -- 'upi_autopay' | 'card' | 'netbanking'
  processor_mandate_id text,
  max_amount_minor int,           -- HARD CAP 499900 (₹4,999)
  state text,                     -- pending|active|paused|revoked|expired|failed
  registered_at timestamptz, valid_until date,
  last_prealert_sent_at timestamptz  -- OUR T-36h notice, before the bank's T-24h
)
-- Mandates CANNOT be updated or moved between subscriptions. A plan change =
-- revoke + re-register. Model that explicitly or you will corrupt state.

entitlements (                    -- the ONE thing gating code reads
  id uuid pk, user_id uuid,
  feature text,                   -- 'daily_windows'|'milan'|'ninety_day'|'pdf'|'ask'
  source text,                    -- 'subscription'|'pass'|'one_time'|'promo'|'admin'
  source_id uuid,
  granted_at timestamptz, expires_at timestamptz,  -- NULL = perpetual
  unique (user_id, feature, source_id)
)
create index on entitlements (user_id, feature, expires_at);

subscription_events (             -- append-only; every webhook + state change
  id uuid pk, subscription_id uuid, user_id uuid,
  event_type text, processor_event_id text unique,  -- webhook idempotency
  payload jsonb, occurred_at timestamptz
)

dunning_attempts (
  id uuid pk, subscription_id uuid, attempt_no int,
  attempted_at timestamptz, result text, failure_code text,
  next_attempt_at timestamptz, channel_notified text
)

-- === THE SCALE LAYER ===
panchang_day (                    -- CITY-scoped. Shared by every user in that city.
  id uuid pk,
  city_key text,                  -- rounded lat/lng grid cell, ~25km
  local_date date,
  sunrise_utc timestamptz, sunset_utc timestamptz,
  tithi text, nakshatra text, yoga text, karana text,
  choghadiya jsonb, rahu_kaal jsonb, hora_schedule jsonb,
  transit_positions jsonb,        -- grahas sampled at 30-min intervals
  computed_at timestamptz,
  unique (city_key, local_date)
)

daily_windows (                   -- USER×DAY. The product.
  id uuid pk, user_id uuid, chart_hash text,
  local_date date, city_key text,
  windows jsonb,                  -- [{start,end,score,polarity,domain,reason,rule_id}]
  headline text, avoid_text text, action_text text,
  day_score numeric, locale text,
  generated_at timestamptz, engine_version text,
  unique (user_id, local_date)
)
create index on daily_windows (user_id, local_date desc);

quiz_sessions (
  id uuid pk, anon_id text, user_id uuid, locale text, source text,
  answers jsonb, current_step int, completed_at timestamptz,
  chart_hash text, primary_domain text, event_date date, event_label text,
  archetype text, own_words text, partner_chart_hash text
)

pricing_config (                  -- prices are DATA. Never compiled in.
  id uuid pk, plan_code text, country_group text, currency text,
  amount_minor int, display_period text, badge text,
  sort_order int, is_default bool, active bool, variant text
)

paywall_variants (id, name, config jsonb, traffic_pct, active)
i18n_strings (key, locale, value, updated_at)   -- DB-backed, not code files
notification_schedule (id, user_id, kind, scheduled_for, channel, locale, state, payload)
```

**Entitlement resolution becomes one function.** Keep `isEntitledPaymentStatus()` in `src/lib/reports/entitlement.ts` for legacy one-time reports; add `resolveEntitlement(userId, feature, at = now())` that reads `entitlements`. Backfill existing paid reports into `entitlements` with `expires_at = null`. Every gate in `src/app/(app)/report/[id]/page.tsx` and the API routes moves to the new function.

**RLS on every new table.** Users select own rows; service role full access. Follow the pattern already used in `20260702_day_ratings.sql`.

---

## 2. DAILY SCORE GENERATION AT SCALE — THE TWO-LAYER TRICK

The naive design calls the Python ephemeris once per user per day. At 10,000 subscribers that is 10,000 calls/night and it will not hold.

**Layer A — city-day (shared).** Panchang, sunrise/sunset, tithi/nakshatra/yoga/karana, choghadiya, rahu kaal, hora schedule, and transit longitudes sampled every 30 minutes depend **only on (city_key, date)**. At 300 city cells that is **300 ephemeris calls per night regardless of user count.** Cache in `panchang_day` (Postgres, immutable once written) + Redis. Precompute **7 days ahead** so a mid-day failure never leaves a user without a window.

**Layer B — user-day (cheap).** The natal chart is immutable and already stored. The full Vimshottari sequence is precomputed at chart creation, so today's dasha is a **lookup, not a computation**. The daily score is then a **pure function of (natal positions, dasha state, Layer A transits, functional nature)** — no ephemeris call, no LLM call, runs in Node in single-digit milliseconds. 10,000 users × 18 windows = 180,000 rows, batched.

**Cost scales with cities × days, not users × days.** That is the whole architecture.

**Copy for the headline/action lines:** template-driven from `rule_id`, translated via `i18n_strings`, with an **LLM pass only on the weekly recap** — never in the nightly per-user path. An LLM call per user per day is both a cost and a latency disaster, and it makes the output unauditable (which kills the "See the calculation" wedge).

**Orchestration — Inngest, already installed:**
- `cron: panchang.precompute` (01:00 IST) → fan-out over active city cells × next 7 days → `panchang_day`
- `cron: windows.generate` (02:00 IST) → fan-out over users with an active `daily_windows` entitlement → batch 500/step, `unique (user_id, local_date)` upsert makes it idempotent
- `cron: notify.dispatch` (every 15 min) → drains `notification_schedule` for the current slot
- `event: subscription.prealert` (T-36h before any debit)
- `event: dunning.retry` (days 1/3/5/7/10)
- `cron: weekly.recap` (Sunday 18:00 local, bucketed by tz)

**Redis (Upstash, already installed):** `dw:{userId}:{date}` today's payload TTL 36h · `pd:{cityKey}:{date}` TTL 7d · `streak:{userId}` · quiz step counters. Postgres remains the source of truth; Redis is read-through only.

---

## 3. WHAT THE STACK MUST CHANGE

**Next.js.** Add a locale segment — routes become `/[locale]/...`. Middleware already sets `x-currency` (`currencyFromHeader` in `src/lib/pricing.ts`); extend it to set `x-locale` and `x-country-group` from `x-vercel-ip-country` + `Accept-Language`. Replace `src/app/(app)/onboarding/page.tsx` (currently a 5-line redirect) with the real `/start` flow. Prices render server-side from `pricing_config`, never from `ZIINA_PLANS`.

**Supabase.** Add the tables above; do not extend `reports`. Move entitlement reads to `resolveEntitlement`. `panchang_day` and `daily_windows` are high-volume — partition `daily_windows` by month once past ~5M rows and drop partitions older than 13 months.

**Python ephemeris.** Two changes: (a) add `POST /panchang-batch` and `POST /transit-series` taking `{city_key, dates[], sample_minutes}` so the nightly job makes ~300 batched calls instead of thousands; (b) **remove it from the per-request path of the daily loop entirely** — it now serves chart creation and the nightly precompute only. Keep it stateless behind Railway autoscale; the sidereal-mode-reasserting middleware in `main.py` already makes it concurrency-safe.

**Payments.** New `src/lib/billing/` replacing `src/lib/ziina/` as the abstraction: `createPass()`, `createSubscription()`, `registerMandate()`, `pauseSubscription()`, `cancelSubscription()`, `handleWebhook()`. Two adapters: `razorpay/` (INR — UPI intent for the Pass, UPI Autopay for mandates) and `stripe/` (diaspora). **Webhooks must be idempotent** on `processor_event_id` and must write to `subscription_events` before mutating `subscriptions` — Indian UPI webhooks retry aggressively.

**Notifications.** `src/lib/notify/whatsapp.ts` becomes the primary channel and needs a WhatsApp Business API provider with **pre-approved message templates per locale** (utility category for the pre-debit notice, marketing for win-back — get templates approved early, it takes days). Add web push: service worker + VAPID, and the install-prompt capture described in the daily loop.

**Analytics.** PostHog is installed. Instrument before launch, not after: `quiz_start{locale,source}` · `quiz_step_view{index,question_id}` · `quiz_step_answer{index,question_id,ms_on_screen}` · `quiz_abandon{index}` · `proof_screen_view` · `proof_confirmed{yes|no}` · `loader_complete` · `recap_view` · `recap_scroll_depth` · `paywall_view{variant}` · `plan_select` · `checkout_start{rail}` · `pass_purchased` · `mandate_created{rail}` · `subscription_started` · `debit_attempt` / `debit_result{code}` · `cancel{day,reason}` · `downsell_view{rung}`. **Headline KPI is revenue per organic session, segmented by country and by cohort (quiz-intent vs SEO-express).** Never a blended conversion rate.

---

## 4. FORKABILITY — pnpm workspace monorepo

```
packages/
  ephemeris-client/   typed client + batch helpers          [domain-agnostic-ish]
  panchang-core/      Layer A, cache-aware                  [Vedic-specific]
  scoring/            natal × transit → windows. RULES LIVE HERE ONLY.
  quiz-engine/        JSON question-graph interpreter       [FULLY GENERIC]
  billing-in/         Razorpay UPI Autopay, RBI pre-alert,
                      dunning, pause/cancel                 [FULLY GENERIC]
  entitlements/       user × feature × time                 [FULLY GENERIC]
  loop/               notification scheduler, streaks,
                      check-ins, weekly recap               [FULLY GENERIC]
  i18n/               DB-backed strings + locale middleware  [FULLY GENERIC]
  ui/                 design tokens + primitives            [themeable]
apps/
  vedichour/
  <parallel-product>/
```

**The fork swaps four things and nothing else:** the quiz JSON graph, the `scoring` package, `i18n_strings` rows, and the `ui` design tokens. It **keeps** billing (the hardest, most India-specific work you will do — reusing it is the entire argument for the monorepo), entitlements, the notification scheduler, the streak/check-in loop, and the paywall/pricing config machinery.

**Two rules that make this real:**
1. **The quiz is data, not code.** A 31-screen flow expressed as a JSON graph of `{id, type, copy_key, options, branch_rules, next}` interpreted by a renderer. A parallel product writes a new JSON file and new `i18n_strings` rows — no React changes. This also lets you cut a question from the admin UI when telemetry says it changes nothing.
2. **Nothing outside `scoring/` and `panchang-core/` may contain the words `dasha`, `nakshatra` or `graha`.** Enforce it with an ESLint rule. The moment Vedic vocabulary leaks into `billing-in/` or `loop/`, the fork stops being free.

**Do this at the start.** Extracting packages from a shipped `src/lib` is a week of pain; starting there costs a day.


---

## 5. Design direction

## THE PROBLEM TO SOLVE

The current site reads as AI-generated. The tells are specific and fixable: centred gradient hero, purple→indigo, glassmorphic cards, generic star field, Inter at every weight, emoji as icons, a three-column feature grid with rounded-square icons, dark mode by default. Every one of those is a default, and defaults are what "AI-generated" actually means.

The replacement is not "more polish." It is **source-specific material**: a printed Indian panchang almanac, and the North Indian square kundli diagram. Both are instantly legible to a 45-year-old Indian user and impossible to mistake for a SaaS template.

---

## PALETTE — paper, not night sky. Light by default.

```
/* Ground */
--paper          #FAF6EF   page background (warm off-white, printed-almanac stock)
--paper-2        #F2EAD9   cards, table stripes
--rule           #E2D6C0   hairlines, table rules, chart borders

/* Ink */
--ink            #1A1512   primary text (near-black, warm — never #000)
--ink-2          #4A423C   secondary text
--ink-3          #8C7A63   sandalwood; captions, metadata, disabled

/* Structure */
--indigo         #1F3A6E   primary accent — panchang ink, headings, links, chart lines
--indigo-deep    #16294D   pressed states, the one dark surface (nav, footer)

/* Signal — the only two colours that carry meaning */
--haldi          #E0A526   FAVOURABLE windows, primary CTA, streak
--haldi-soft     #F5E3B8   favourable band fill
--sindoor        #C1361F   AVOID windows, errors
--sindoor-soft   #F0D5CE   avoid band fill

/* Dark mode (secondary, not default) */
--paper          #14110E
--paper-2        #1E1A16
--ink            #F4EEE3
--indigo         #7FA3D9   (lightened for AA on dark)
--haldi          #F0BC4A
```

**Discipline rules.** Haldi and sindoor mean *exactly* favourable and avoid — never decoration, never a button that isn't a primary action. Indigo carries all structure. Sandalwood carries all de-emphasis. **No green.** Green/red is the generic dashboard signature and it fights the material. Gold-vs-terracotta is both more Indian and more distinctive, and it reads correctly to colour-blind users when paired with the pattern fill below.

**Accessibility:** never encode favourable/avoid in colour alone — favourable bands are solid fill, avoid bands are diagonal-hatched. This is also how printed panchangs mark them, so the accessibility fix *is* the authentic detail.

**Light by default, deliberately.** Every astrology app on the store is a dark star field. A daylight paper ground is the fastest way to look like nothing else in the category — and it is what a physical panchang looks like.

---

## TYPE PAIRING

| Role | Latin | Devanagari |
|---|---|---|
| **Display / headings** | **Newsreader** (700/600) | **Tiro Devanagari Hindi** |
| **UI / body** | **Instrument Sans** (400/500/600) | **Mukta** (400/500/600) |
| **Times, scores, tables** | **IBM Plex Mono** (tabular figures) | IBM Plex Mono |

**Why.** Newsreader and Tiro Devanagari Hindi both carry real editorial character — Tiro is a proper Devanagari serif designed for extended reading, not a system fallback, and default system Devanagari is a major reason Indian products read as cheap. Instrument Sans is a workhorse with more personality than Inter and is not the AI default. **IBM Plex Mono with tabular figures is non-negotiable**: this is a timing product, times and scores appear in vertical columns, and proportional figures make them jitter. A monospaced clock also visually asserts *computation* rather than *vibes* — which is exactly the positioning.

**Sizing for the audience.** Devanagari body at **17–18px / 1.7 line-height**, left-aligned, never centred, never justified. Hindi strings run 15–25% longer than English — every button, card and table cell must be flexible-width, never fixed. Test at 360px in daylight on a mid-range Android; that is the actual usage condition.

**Numerals.** Indian digit grouping throughout — `1,84,203` not `184,203`. Getting this wrong signals "built by someone who isn't from here" faster than anything else on the page.

---

## ILLUSTRATION DIRECTION

**The recurring motif is the North Indian square kundli chart** — the diamond-in-a-square, drawn as clean SVG with the user's own planets placed in it. Not a decorative zodiac wheel. Show it early in the quiz, at the loader, and on the recap. It is the single element that does more for credibility with a 30–60 Indian audience than any amount of polish, and no AI generates it unprompted.

**Panchang table typography as a design system.** Hairline rules, alternating `--paper-2` stripes, tabular figures, dense but airy — the layout language of a printed almanac. Use it for the daily windows, the dasha timeline, and the "See the calculation" panel. This turns your transparency wedge into a visual identity.

**The nine grahas: single-weight line drawings with flat fills**, commissioned or hand-drawn, in indigo on paper with one haldi accent. Traditional iconography (Shani's crow, Chandra's crescent), stylised flat, not devotional-realistic and not 3D gradient orbs. Nine drawings is a bounded, affordable commission and it is the highest-leverage design spend on the list.

**Texture:** a subtle paper grain at ~3% opacity on the ground. One asymmetric editorial layout on the home screen — visible grid, generous left margin, content that does not sit in a centred 640px column.

**Banned outright:** gradient heroes, glassmorphism, star fields, purple, emoji as UI icons, 3D-rendered planets, stock "mystical woman looking at the cosmos," centred everything, dark mode as the default.

**The test:** strip all colour from a screen. If it still looks like every other landing page, the *layout* is the problem, not the palette.

---

## MULTILINGUAL APPROACH

**Localisation is the highest-win-rate experiment category in the entire dataset (Adapty: 62.3% LTV win rate vs 34.6% for visual redesigns).** It is a conversion lever, not an accessibility feature. It also outranks the pricing work on probability of success.

**Ship order:**
1. **English + Hinglish (Latin script)** at launch. Hinglish is a genuinely distinct register for the 30–60 mobile audience and often outperforms both pure English and pure Hindi. Write it as spoken — *"Aapka Shukra strong hai, paise ke liye achha time aa raha hai"* — not as transliterated formal Hindi. **Zero font or rendering risk**, which is why it ships first.
2. **Hindi (Devanagari)** second, with Tiro/Mukta and a full text-expansion pass.
3. **Marathi, Bengali, Telugu, Tamil** in the order your own analytics justify.

**Non-negotiables:**
- **No machine translation.** Machine-translated Hindi in a spiritual product reads as inauthentic to exactly the audience you are targeting and damages credibility more than English would. Human-written per locale, reviewed by a native speaker who is also the target age.
- **Sanskrit-derived terms stay untranslated in every locale** — muhurat, dasha, antardasha, gochar, panchang, nakshatra, kundli, milan, graha. They are already the audience's native vocabulary; Englishing them weakens credibility. Gloss once, then use freely.
- **Language is screen 0 of the quiz**, before any question. One tap, and it signals respect before you ask for anything.
- **Locale is a first-class column** on the user, inherited by every push, WhatsApp message, email, PDF and paywall — not a settings toggle.
- **Strings live in `i18n_strings` in Supabase**, not in code files, so copy can be edited without a deploy, A/B tested per locale, and forked into a parallel product by copying rows.
- **Design for expansion from day one.** Hindi runs 15–25% longer. Fixed-width buttons will break, and they will break in exactly the markets you are trying to win.


---

## 6. Build order

| # | Item | Effort | Unlocks |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ten recorded user sessions with real Indian users pulled off live traffic (₹500 for 20 min; ≥6 Hindi-first, ≥6 mobile) — watch them hit the current paywall and REFUSE | S | The cheapest and highest-information item on the list. You have traffic and zero customers, which is a diagnosis you have not actually made yet. Ask what they'd pay, what they currently pay for astrology, and whether they would ever enter card details or only UPI. This can invalidate half of this document before you spend a month building it. Run two of these a week permanently. |
| 2 | Razorpay merchant application: Indian entity, GST, current account, and WRITTEN astrology-category approval for recurring UPI Autopay mandates | S | Calendar time, not build time — which is why it is rank 2 and not rank 8. Approval for occult/astrology recurring mandates can take weeks and can be refused. Email your account manager on day one with the exact use case and get a written yes. Name Cashfree as the fallback in the same week. Every other item below is worthless if this fails, and you will not find out for three weeks. |
| 3 | pnpm monorepo extraction: packages/{ephemeris-client, panchang-core, scoring, quiz-engine, billing-in, entitlements, loop, i18n, ui} + apps/vedichour | M | Forkability, at a cost of one day now versus a week of pain later. Add the ESLint rule that bans Vedic vocabulary outside scoring/ and panchang-core/ on the same day — the discipline only holds if it is mechanical. |
| 4 | Entitlements refactor: entitlements + subscriptions + payment_mandates + subscription_events tables, resolveEntitlement(userId, feature, at), backfill existing paid reports | M | Every gate in the product. Nothing recurring can ship while entitlement lives on reports.payment_status. Keeps src/lib/reports/entitlement.ts working for legacy one-time reports so nothing breaks. |
| 5 | pricing_config table + kill ZIINA_PLANS as source of truth; geo-detected ladders (IN / diaspora / RoW) rendered server-side from DB | S | The ability to ever discover the right Indian price. Puff Count A/B-tested $4→$12 without a deploy. Also unlocks the ~10x diaspora ARPU spread at zero product cost — arguably the single highest-margin change in the plan. |
| 6 | billing-in package: Razorpay Orders (₹99 Pass, UPI intent, no mandate) + Razorpay Subscriptions (UPI Autopay mandate, ₹4,999 cap) + idempotent webhooks writing to subscription_events | L | Revenue. This is the critical path, not the quiz. A beautiful 31-screen funnel that dead-ends at a card form in a market with ~8% credit-card penetration converts at zero regardless of the copy. Ship the ₹99 Pass path first and the mandate path second — the Pass earns money on day one and needs no mandate at all. |
| 7 | Two-layer daily generation: natal_charts + panchang_day + daily_windows tables, /panchang-batch and /transit-series endpoints on the Python service, Inngest panchang.precompute and windows.generate crons | L | The product itself, and the only design that survives growth: ephemeris cost scales with cities × days (~300 calls/night) instead of users × days. Precompute 7 days ahead so a failed night is invisible. No LLM in the nightly per-user path. |
| 8 | quiz-engine: JSON question-graph interpreter + the 31 screens at /start, with the verifiable-past-hit proof screen (S11b) and the 14-second real-computation loader | L | The funnel — and because the graph is data, the ability to cut or reorder questions from telemetry without a deploy. The proof screen (naming the dasha that was running when a change they just confirmed happened) is the highest-conviction screen in the whole design and the thing no sun-sign competitor can imitate. |
| 9 | Recap screen (8 blocks, animated 90-day projection chart with their event flagged) + paywall (₹99 Pass primary, annual below fold, UPI-first, honesty strip) + 4-rung downsell ladder | M | Conversion. Rung 3 — the ₹199 dated answer titled from their own Q12 event — monetizes the 85–95% who will never subscribe, which is how India actually buys astrology. The projection chart is the single asset the paywall sells; build it properly and animate it. |
| 10 | Daily loop: home screen (24h band, three lines, See-the-calculation), morning WhatsApp/push at the Q13 slot, evening check-in wired to the EXISTING /api/day-rating + day_ratings table, streak on windows-used, Sunday weekly alignment recap | M | Retention, the day-11 upgrade evidence, and the renewal justification — one mechanic serving all three. Cheaper than it looks: supabase/migrations/20260702_day_ratings.sql already ships the table with predicted_score snapshotting, and src/lib/notify/whatsapp.ts already exists. The weekly recap that ADMITS the day it got wrong is the moat. |
| 11 | i18n package: locale middleware + /[locale] routes + i18n_strings table + human-written Hinglish and English strings across quiz, paywall, notifications and PDFs | M | The highest-win-rate experiment category in the dataset (62.3% LTV win rate; HDFC: 2x conversions at 40% lower CPA). There is currently zero i18n anywhere in the codebase. Hinglish first because it carries no font or rendering risk; Devanagari after, with a text-expansion pass. |
| 12 | Trial→subscription sequence (day 0/1/3/7/11/13/14/21), T-36h pre-debit notice ahead of the bank's T-24h RBI alert, dunning ladder days 1/3/5/7/10 on WhatsApp, pause + two-tap cancel | M | Actual collected revenue rather than nominal conversions. 31% of Play cancellations are involuntary billing failures; ~90% of dunning recoveries land in the first 10 days; 35% of annual cancellations happen in month 1. Pre-empting the bank's own cancel prompt is a trust move nobody in this category makes — and requires WhatsApp utility templates approved in advance, so start that with rank 2. |
| 13 | PWA completion: service worker, VAPID web push, install-prompt capture fired on the first evening check-in confirmation (day 1, ~9pm) | S | iOS push (which requires home-screen install) and a permanent home-screen surface. src/app/manifest.ts already exists — this is the missing half. Matters most for the diaspora tier, where iOS share is high and ARPU is ~10x. |
| 14 | Design system rebuild: paper palette, Newsreader/Tiro/Instrument Sans/Mukta/Plex Mono, square kundli motif, panchang table typography, nine commissioned graha line drawings | M | Not looking AI-generated, which is a trust problem in a category where trust is the entire purchase. Ranked here deliberately — it should NOT block revenue, but it must land before you spend on traffic. The nine graha drawings are the highest-leverage single spend. |
| 15 | Hindi programmatic SEO layer under /hi/: rashifal, panchang-by-city (top 300), kundli-milan pairs, nakshatra, dasha explainers — plus the 3-question express widget on the seven EXISTING calculator routes | L | Compounding free traffic with no CPA, using the ephemeris you already own as a content generator. AstroTalk's Hindi subdirectory alone drives 250k+ monthly visits off a keyword set you can address. The express widget matters more than the new pages: a 15-question wall in front of 'aaj ka rashifal' intent destroys the one asset the business currently has. Follow Lean SEO — publish, do not touch for 30 days, then scale only what ranked. |
| 16 | Experiment harness: paywall_variants, PostHog step-level telemetry on every quiz screen, revenue-per-organic-session dashboards split by country and by cohort (quiz vs SEO-express) | M | The revenue curve itself. Cal AI ran 123 experiments / 424 variants at ~5/month for 3x revenue in 10 months; apps running 14+ tests a year earn up to 40x more. Sequence by measured win rate: localisation → trial structure → plan duration → pricing → question order → visuals last. Everything above is hypothesis one. |
| 17 | Diaspora tier: Stripe adapter, USD/GBP/AUD ladder ($4.99 Pass / $59.99 yr / $9.99 mo), English-only, LUT export-of-services or separate entity decided | M | Roughly 10x ARPU per user on traffic you already receive and currently monetize identically to Indian traffic. Sidesteps RBI mandate machinery entirely. Deliberately after the India rail — but if Razorpay astrology approval is refused at rank 2, this becomes rank 3 and the business pivots to diaspora-led. |
| 18 | Marriage Decision Plan (₹2,999 / 3 months, human-reviewed kundli milan) surfaced only on the marriage branch | M | The only place four-figure pricing is defensible in India — Shaadi.com Gold Plus runs ≈₹1,090/mo against exactly this intent, and 60% of AstroTalk's revenue is marriage-related. Requires a human in the loop, so it is a service-ops build, not a software build. Do not start it until the subscription converts. |

---

## 7. Open risks (ranked)

1. PRICING IS STILL THE MOST LIKELY KILLER, EVEN AFTER THE CORRECTION. ₹1,999/mo was off by ~16x against your own current ₹1,499 one-time price and ~25x against Sri Mandir's ₹600-800 ANNUAL Indian ARPU. I have moved it to ₹1,999/year — but there is no proven mass-market Indian astrology subscription at ANY price to point to. AstroTalk built ₹1,214 Cr with 90-95% of revenue from prepaid per-minute wallets; Marc Lou killed three products with recurring pricing (IndiePage: $4,000/mo at $45 lifetime vs $0/mo at $15/year). The honest possibility is that recurring is the wrong shape for this audience entirely and the real business is the ₹99 Pass plus the ₹199 dated answer, sold repeatedly. Mitigation: ship the one-time paths on the same paywall, measure blended revenue per session, and let the data decide rather than defending the subscription thesis.
2. RAZORPAY MAY REFUSE OR SLOW-WALK ASTROLOGY RECURRING MANDATES, AND YOU WILL NOT KNOW FOR WEEKS. Occult/astrology sits in the restricted or enhanced-scrutiny bucket for most Indian payment aggregators. If UPI Autopay for this category is refused, the entire recurring model collapses to one-time payments and the business becomes diaspora-led (Stripe) plus Indian one-time. This is unknowable from research and must be resolved by a written answer from your account manager in week one. It is the single longest-lead, highest-consequence unknown in the plan — which is why it is build item #2, not #8.
3. THE ENTITY AND TAX PREREQUISITES MAY NOT EXIST AT ALL. Razorpay Subscriptions requires a registered Indian entity, PAN, current account and KYC. GST registration at 18% is mandatory and eats ~15% of every rupee (₹1,999 nets ~₹1,694). The diaspora USD tier needs an LUT-backed export-of-services setup or a separate non-Indian entity, with FIRC handling. If the business is currently operating without an Indian entity, the pivot is blocked on incorporation, not on code — and no amount of engineering velocity compensates.
4. RBI HAS LEGISLATED AWAY THE PASSIVE-CONVERSION REVENUE THE ENTIRE WESTERN PLAYBOOK DEPENDS ON. Every Indian subscriber's own bank must send a pre-debit alert 24h before every auto-debit with one-tap cancel. The ₹99 Pass design dodges this during evaluation, but it hits every renewal forever. Combined with ~70% recurring-card decline rates observed after the card e-mandate rollout and 31% involuntary cancellation rates on Play, collected revenue will materially trail nominal conversions. Any model assuming Western renewal retention will overstate revenue, possibly by half.
5. THE 60% PAYWALL-CONVERSION TARGET IS NOT REACHABLE AND WILL MISPRICE ACQUISITION BY 3-4x IF HELD. RevenueCat puts India+SEA trial-to-paid at 15.2% — the worst region measured — and D35 download-to-paid at 1.37%. The 60% figure that circulates is top-quartile, in the best-converting regions, on funnels with hundreds of experiments behind them. Plan on quiz-complete→Pass of 25-35% and Pass→subscription of 35-45%, and hold them as SEPARATE instrumented numbers. A single blended figure will hide which half is broken.
6. EXECUTION CAPACITY, NOT STRATEGY, IS THE MOST PROBABLE CAUSE OF FAILURE. This document contains eighteen workstreams. The documented failure mode across the entire founder library is building instead of distributing — one founder shipped 72 apps in 60 days for ₹0. If more than three of these run in parallel, the likely outcome is a half-built funnel, a half-built billing rail, and no revenue. The enforced sequence is: user interviews and Razorpay application → entitlements and billing → quiz and paywall → daily loop → and nothing else until revenue per session moves off zero.
7. THE ₹99 PAID PASS IS THE BIGGEST UNTESTED BET IN THIS DOCUMENT. Every benchmark cited (48.8% opt-out vs 18.2% opt-in) is for FREE card-required trials, not paid micro-trials, and none of it is from India. A paid trial will cut top-of-funnel volume relative to free, and the magnitude is unknown. The argument for it is structural rather than empirical — it dodges card penetration, the RBI cancel prompt, and the complaint pattern that destroys every incumbent — but it is a reasoned bet, not a measured one. Experiment #1 after launch must be ₹0 vs ₹49 vs ₹99 vs ₹149, and you must be willing to be wrong about it.
8. BIRTH-TIME-UNKNOWN AT Q4 IS THE LARGEST SINGLE DROP-OFF RISK AND HAS NO ANALOGUE IN THE DIET APPS THIS PLAYBOOK COMES FROM. A large share of the 30-60 Indian audience does not know their birth time. If the reassurance screen reads as 'your result will be worse,' you lose the biggest recoverable cohort at screen 6 of 31 — before the free payoff, before the proof screen, before anything. This must be designed and user-tested as a feature, not patched as an edge case.
9. THE VERIFIABLE-PAST-HIT PROOF SCREEN IS THE STRONGEST IDEA IN THIS SPEC AND ALSO THE MOST FRAGILE. It works only when the computed dasha change lands near a real event in the user's life. For users with no memorable change in that window, the screen fires blank and the flow loses its centre of gravity at exactly the wrong moment. It needs a graceful degradation path (offer adjacent years, fall back to a transit-based hit, or route to the free Moon-sign payoff) and it needs testing on at least 20 real charts before it ships. Do not build the recap's credibility on a screen that misses a third of the time.
10. THE WEEKLY ALIGNMENT SCORE IS SIMULTANEOUSLY THE BEST RETENTION MECHANIC AND A LEGAL AND REPUTATIONAL LIABILITY. Publishing 'we called 5 of 7 days right' makes the product falsifiable, invites refund demands, and creates a public criticism surface no competitor exposes. The existing day_ratings migration already sets the safer convention (alignment and reflection, never 'prediction accuracy proof') — keep it, and get the guarantee copy and the refund promise reviewed by an Indian consumer-law lawyer before either ships.
11. ASTROLOGICAL GUIDANCE OVER HEALTH, LEGAL AND FINANCIAL DECISIONS CAN CAUSE REAL HARM. An 'avoid this window' over a medical procedure or a court date is not a neutral statement, and Q7 explicitly collects those intents. A content policy with hard exclusions — no medical deferral advice, no specific investment instruction, no litigation-outcome prediction — must be built into the generation rules themselves, not bolted on as a footer disclaimer. This is also the surface most likely to attract consumer-protection scrutiny or processor review.
12. MACHINE-TRANSLATED HINDI WOULD DAMAGE CREDIBILITY MORE THAN ENGLISH DOES. Localisation is the highest-win-rate lever available, but only if it is genuinely native. Machine-translated devotional or astrological Hindi reads as fake to exactly the audience being targeted, and Devanagari carries 15-25% text expansion plus font-rendering issues on the mid-range Android devices this demographic actually uses. Hinglish-first is the risk-managed path; budget for a human writer per locale or do not ship the locale.
13. THE 'AI ASTROLOGER' AND EVEN THE 'SHOW OUR WORKING' WEDGES ARE NARROWER THAN THEY LOOK. AstroSage already ships a talking AI astrologer in every Indian language with a free minute; InstaAstro raised $12M to build the same. The transparency wedge — showing the dasha, the transit and the rule — is real but cheaply copied once someone notices it works. The durable moat is the accumulated per-user check-in history and the alignment score computed from it, which takes months of subscriber-days to build and cannot be cloned. Everything else buys you a head start, not a defence.
14. SEO AND QUIZ TRAFFIC ARE DIFFERENT COHORTS AND MIXING THEM WILL CORRUPT EVERY READ YOU TAKE. Someone arriving from 'aaj ka rashifal' has informational intent and will convert at a fraction of quiz-intent traffic. If they share a funnel and a dashboard, you will conclude the funnel is broken when it is the cohort mix that changed. The 3-question express path plus separate end-to-end cohort tracking is not optional instrumentation — it is what makes the numbers mean anything.
15. SEVERAL FIGURES UNDERPINNING THESE DECISIONS COME FROM THIRD-PARTY TEARDOWNS AND AGGREGATORS RATHER THAN PRIMARY DATA. Screen counts, revenue estimates, the opt-in/opt-out trial benchmarks and some vernacular conversion ranges are directionally reliable but not exact. RevenueCat, Adapty, Stripe, TechCrunch and BW Disrupt figures are primary or near-primary and should be weighted higher. More importantly, none of these medians include Indian astrology as a category — every number in this document is a prior to be tested, not a target to be hit, which is precisely why remote pricing config and a fixed experiment cadence matter more than picking the right initial values.

---

## Appendix — source research

### quiz-paywall-mechanics

**Scope:** Research into the adaptive onboarding-quiz → paywall pattern used by consumer health/habit subscription apps, and its adaptation to VedicHour's pivot to a card/mandate-required trial subscription for an Indian, mobile-first, mixed-language audience aged ~30-60. Covered: (1) quiz length, question order and ordering logic; (2) the psychological device catalogue; (3) paywall placement and anatomy; (4) trial mechanics and published conversion benchmarks, including India-specific ones; (5) the recap/"your plan is ready" screen. Products studied for structure: Noom, Cal AI, Yazio, Fabulous, Fastic, BetterMe, Impulse, Elevate, RISE, Quitzilla, Lose It!, Simple, Me+, Duolingo, plus astrology-category comparables Nebula and Co-Star and the Indian incumbent AstroTalk. Benchmarks drawn from RevenueCat State of Subscription Apps 2026 (115,000+ apps, $16B revenue), Adapty (16,000+ apps / $3B revenue), and Business of Apps. Also scanned the existing VedicHour codebase (C:\Users\user\OneDrive\Documents\Projects\astrology\jyotish-ai) to check what the quiz/paywall would plug into. Not covered: primary A/B data from VedicHour's own traffic (none available to me), and Zoe/Sleep Cycle, for which no credible teardown surfaced — Sleep Cycle is a one-time-purchase model and is not a valid template here.
