  - Evidence: A LocalCircles survey conducted 1 December 2023 to 30 January 2024, with over 44,000 responses across 331 districts of India, found 67% of consumers who bought a product or service via an app or SaaS platform said it turned out to be a subscription trap, and 71% reported hidden charges not presented upfront. Reported practices included auto-renewals without consent, hidden cancellation buttons and multi-step cancellation, 'often following a free trial that converts to paid service' (Business Standard; BestMediaInfo).
  - So what: Asking a 45-year-old Hindi-speaking user for a UPI Autopay mandate mid-funnel will read as a trap unless the amount, the date and the cancel path are stated on the same screen in their own language. Transparency here is not just compliance — it is the conversion lever.
- **The regulatory direction of travel is tightening, not loosening.**
  - Evidence: RBI released a draft on 11 February 2026, applying to commercial banks and proposed to take effect from 1 July 2026, which for the first time explicitly defines and prohibits dark patterns in sectoral banking rules (Medianama). The CCPA advisory of 5 June 2025 runs through 31 December 2026. The 2026 E-mandate Framework itself added a mandatory post-transaction notification carrying grievance-redressal details, on top of the pre-debit notification.
  - So what: Any design that depends on friction, obscurity or inertia has a shrinking shelf life in India. Build the compliant version now rather than retrofitting under enforcement pressure.
- **Mandate amounts are fixed at registration and cannot be raised without re-authorisation — a price rise later would break every existing subscriber.**
  - Evidence: The 2026 Framework accommodates fixed- and variable-amount mandates, with the customer specifying the maximum permissible value of any individual recurring transaction. Paddle documents the operational consequence: when a subscription change increases the mandate amount, the next renewal fails and the subscription becomes `past_due`, requiring the customer to authorise a new mandate (RBI framework via IndiaLaw; Paddle Developer Docs, UPI).
  - So what: Set the mandate maximum above the launch price from day one (e.g. register the mandate at a ₹999 or ₹1,499 ceiling while charging ₹399), so introductory-to-standard step-ups and future increases do not require re-collecting consent from the entire base.
- **Cost of the recurring stack is a rounding error compared to success-rate differences — do not optimise for MDR.**
  - Evidence: Razorpay's published domestic rate is 2% + 18% GST, with a reported additional subscriptions fee of about 0.99% on recurring card payments. Cashfree charges ₹5 per e-mandate creation and ₹5 per debit, with UPI AutoPay at ₹5+ per debit and a ₹4,999/year AMC. Razorpay's own analysis notes that a payment success-rate variance of 5–7 percentage points has a larger rupee impact than a 0.2% TDR difference, and that ₹4,999/year AMC adds ₹416 to monthly operating costs, with the break-even against a 0.25% lower TDR at roughly ₹1.67 lakh monthly GMV (Razorpay pricing/recurring-billing blogs; Cashfree e-mandate pricing; Medium/Refrens gateway comparisons).
  - So what: Choose the processor on category acceptance, UPI Autopay Intent quality and dunning tooling, then negotiate rate. At VedicHour's current volume the MDR difference between Razorpay, Cashfree and PayU is immaterial.

**Apply to VedicHour**

- **Kill the card-required free trial. Replace it with a paid ₹1 introductory period on a UPI Autopay mandate. This is the single change that makes the whole model legal in India.**
  - Mechanic: user completes the quiz, sees the paywall, taps 'Start' → Razorpay Subscriptions creates a subscription with an authorisation transaction of ₹1 (or ₹9) charged immediately as an `addons` upfront amount, and `start_at` set 7 days in the future. The UPI Autopay mandate is registered at that moment via UPI PIN, with a mandate maximum of ₹999. First full debit lands on day 8.
    
    Why this and not a free trial: CCPA Dark Patterns Guidelines 2023, Annexure-1 item 5 makes it a 'subscription trap' to force a user to provide payment details or auto-debit authorisation to avail a FREE subscription. A ₹1 first week is a paid subscription at an introductory price, which is outside that clause. Never use the words 'free trial' / 'मुफ्त ट्रायल' anywhere a mandate is required — call it 'पहला हफ्ता ₹1' / '₹1 for your first 7 days'.
    
    Exact mandate-screen copy (Hindi): 'आज ₹1. 8वें दिन से ₹399/माह. कभी भी रद्द करें — 2 टैप में.' English: 'Today ₹1. From day 8, ₹399/month. Cancel anytime in 2 taps.' All three facts — today's amount, the recurring amount and date, and the cancel path — must sit on the same screen as the UPI PIN request.
- **Make UPI Autopay via the Intent deep-link flow the default and only primary path on mobile. Cards are a secondary rail, not the gate.**
  - Checkout priority order:
    1. Mobile web (≈90% of traffic): UPI Autopay, Intent flow — deep-link straight into GPay/PhonePe/Paytm. Benchmarked at ~92–95% mandate-creation success. No card fields anywhere on screen.
    2. Desktop: UPI Autopay via QR (scan with phone). Do NOT default to the Collect flow — NPCI has been deprecating manual Collect on mobile and it converts materially worse.
    3. Card e-mandate: shown as 'Pay by card instead' for the ~123M credit-card holders and NRI traffic. Requires 3DS at registration; Razorpay handles tokenization (merchants have been barred from storing raw card data since 1 Oct 2022).
    4. Escape hatch: a one-time '1 month, no auto-renew — ₹449' button for the mandate-averse. Prices slightly above the recurring plan so the mandate is the value choice.
    
    Hard constraint: every recurring amount must stay under ₹15,000, including the annual plan. Above that, every single cycle requires a fresh OTP and UPI Autopay stops being available entirely.
- **Replace Ziina immediately with Razorpay. Ziina physically cannot run this business.**
  - Ziina's entire published API surface is Payment Intent, Transfer, Refund and Webhooks — no subscriptions, no mandates, no saved cards, no tokenization — and it settles only in AED, treating INR as a presentment currency it converts at payment time. It cannot register an RBI e-mandate, cannot emit pre-debit notifications, and cannot legally hold an Indian card on file.
    
    Razorpay is the recommendation because it is the only processor that is simultaneously (a) generally available without an invite, unlike Stripe India; (b) willing to serve astrology — AstroTalk is a published Razorpay case study; and (c) fully compliant out of the box, initiating every subscription debit 24 hours in advance and auto-refunding the ₹5 token authorisation.
    
    Do not use Paddle or Lemon Squeezy. Paddle's Acceptable Use Policy prohibited category 14 expressly bans 'digital services associated with pseudo-science, including but not limited to clairvoyance, horoscopes, fortune-telling'. Paddle is otherwise a perfect technical fit — UPI AutoPay, GST handled as MoR, no Indian entity — which makes it a tempting trap. Onboarding there risks termination and frozen balances after launch.
- **Incorporate an Indian Private Limited company as the merchant of record. Use Razorpay's cross-border Import Flow on the UAE entity only as a 4–8 week bridge while incorporation completes.**
  - Target state — Indian Pvt Ltd. Prerequisites, in order:
    1. Private Limited incorporation with at least one director resident in India (182+ days in the financial year); the board cannot be all-foreign. DIN required for the foreign director.
    2. 100% FDI under the automatic route for this sector; file Form FC-GPR on the FIRMS portal within 30 days of share issue, at a FEMA-compliant valuation with FIRC and bank KYC.
    3. Company PAN, current account, GST registration (18% on the subscription).
    4. Website must carry, before Razorpay activation: Terms & Conditions, Refund/Cancellation Policy, Privacy Policy, contact details with a real address and phone, and a public pricing page showing the recurring amount and frequency.
    
    Bridge state — UAE entity on Razorpay Import Flow. Razorpay's 'Accept International Payments From Indian Customers' lets a foreign business collect INR via UPI, UPI AutoPay, card recurring, e-mandate and netbanking with no Indian entity, settling into an overseas account, with Razorpay handling mandate registration, tokenization and pre-debit notifications. Confirm three things in writing with Razorpay sales before building on it: (a) AED settlement is available (the docs list AED, one Razorpay blog omits it); (b) actual pricing, which is case-by-case; (c) that UPI AutoPay specifically is enabled for the import flow, not just one-time UPI.
    
    Either way, register for Indian GST as an OIDAR supplier if you keep the UAE entity: registration is mandatory regardless of turnover, 18% IGST applies, an Indian representative must be appointed, and GSTR-5A is filed monthly. The non-business/individual exemption was withdrawn on 1 October 2023. The offshore entity saves nothing on tax and adds per-transaction import paperwork and PAN collection friction at checkout.
- **Reprice the entire ladder downward by roughly 5x on the monthly plan, and move ₹1,999 to the annual tier.**
  - Proposed ladder (all charm-priced to Indian convention — ₹99/₹199/₹399/₹999/₹1,499/₹2,999):
    
    • Light — ₹199/month, or ₹1,499/year (framed as '₹125/month, billed yearly')
    • Full — ₹399/month, or ₹2,999/year (framed as '₹249/month, billed yearly')
    • Partner/Compatibility add-on — ₹149/month or ₹999/year
    • Intro period — ₹1 for 7 days into Full
    
    Grounding: Netflix India runs ₹149–649/month; JioHotstar's 2026 monthly plans are ₹79/₹149/₹299 with a ₹1,499 annual; India OTT ARPU is ₹50–180; a household's entire 3–4 service OTT stack costs ₹500–800/month. RevenueCat puts India/SEA 14-day revenue per install at $0.06–0.09 median against $0.39 for North America. ₹1,999/month asks one astrology product to cost 3x a household's whole streaming budget.
    
    But do not conclude Indians won't pay: AstroTalk booked ₹1,214 crore total income in FY25 across ~1.5M transacting users — roughly ₹8,000 per transacting user per year. The willingness to pay is real; it is structured as episodic depth, not flat monthly. So capture it as annual plans plus a partner add-on plus occasional consult credits, and push annual hard — annual plans retain at a 44.1% year-1 median versus 17.0% for monthly.
    
    Register every UPI mandate with a ₹999 (Light) / ₹1,499 (Full) maximum even though you charge less, so a later price rise doesn't force every subscriber to re-authorise.
- **Treat the mandatory 24-hour pre-debit bank notification as a monthly retention moment you own, not a leak you absorb.**
  - You cannot suppress it. Every month, ~24–26 hours before the debit, the issuer sends the customer an SMS/email naming VedicHour, the amount, the debit date and an opt-out link — and Razorpay initiates the debit 24 hours in advance precisely to satisfy this.
    
    So pre-empt it. Send your own WhatsApp message 2–3 hours BEFORE the bank's notification lands, in the user's chosen language, structured as:
    1. Value delivered, specific and countable: 'इस महीने आपने 18 शुभ मुहूर्त देखे' / 'You checked 18 auspicious windows this month.'
    2. What's coming: the single most notable transit in the next 30 days, named.
    3. The charge, stated plainly before the bank states it: 'कल ₹399 कटेंगे.'
    4. A 'Pause for a month' link — not 'Cancel'.
    
    Also: set the billing anchor to day 5–10 of the month, away from month-end salary crunch, to reduce insufficient-balance failures — over 20 million UPI mandates are revoked nationally each month for exactly that reason.
- **Build cancellation to be strictly as easy as signup, and lead the cancel flow with Pause.**
  - Compliance floor set by CCPA Annexure-1 item 5 and the enforcement doctrine that a pre-selected default is not consent:
    • Cancel must be self-serve, in-app, reachable in the same or fewer taps than subscribing. No phone call, no email-only, no 'contact support'.
    • Opt-in and opt-out language must be equally prominent and neutral. McAfee was penalised for a renewal screen offering 'Renew Now' vs 'Accept Risk'.
    • No pre-ticked add-ons (Zepto, PharmEasy, SpiceJet were all penalised for this), no confirm-shaming, no drip pricing, no false urgency countdowns on the paywall.
    • Trial-to-paid conversion must never happen without notification — that is Annexure-1 item 12, 'SaaS billing'.
    
    Cancel-flow order: 'Pause 1 month' → 'Pause 3 months' → 'Switch to Light (₹199)' → 'Cancel'. UPI Autopay supports pausing a mandate, and pause preserves the relationship where cancel destroys it. Instrument the choice split and the stated reason.
- **Ship a WhatsApp-first dunning system on day one. Involuntary churn will be your largest single revenue leak, not voluntary cancellation.**
  - Model assumptions to plan against: UPI Autopay debit failure of 8–15% per cycle (vs 2–3% for cards); success ~85% in month 1 decaying to ~70% by month 6; 68% of global subscription churn is involuntary.
    
    Build:
    • Retry ladder handled by Razorpay, plus your own day-1 WhatsApp with a one-tap one-time UPI payment link for the exact amount — day-1 WhatsApp recovers ~30% versus under 10% for email-only.
    • A 7-day grace period before any feature is locked. Do not hard-lock on first failure.
    • A balance nudge sent the morning of the debit date to users who have failed before.
    • Separate dashboards for voluntary vs involuntary churn. Conflating them will make you cut price when the real problem was a failed debit.
    
    Dunning recovers roughly 85% of involuntary churn when done properly — at ₹399/month across a few thousand subscribers this is the difference between viable and not.
- **Reset the funnel targets. 60%+ paywall conversion is not a plan; here is what to actually instrument.**
  - Grounded targets, stage by stage:
    • Quiz start → quiz complete: 55–70% (this is the lever the adaptive quiz actually controls)
    • Quiz complete → paywall view: 90%+
    • Paywall view → ₹1 intro start: 8–15%. A card wall would put this at the low end or below; UPI Autopay with no card fields is what buys the upper end.
    • ₹1 intro → paid month 1: 30–50%. Opt-out (instrument-required) trials benchmark at ~31.4%; RevenueCat's 2025 trial-to-paid median is ~40% with a 68.3% top decile. Anything above 50% would be genuinely exceptional.
    • Month 1 → month 12, monthly plan: ~17% median. Annual plan: ~44.1%.
    
    Therefore: model the business on annual conversion. Offer the annual upgrade at the end of the ₹1 week and again at month 2, not at first signup. Longer intro periods convert better (45.7% median for 17–32 days vs 26.8% for shorter), so test a 14-day ₹9 intro against the 7-day ₹1 intro before settling.
- **Ship the full disclosure and notification set that both Indian law and card-network rules require, in the user's own language.**
  - At the mandate screen (same screen as UPI PIN / 3DS): recurring amount, frequency, first debit date, mandate maximum, explicit statement that it auto-renews, and a visible 'How to cancel' link.
    
    Notification sequence (mirrors Paddle's own compliance touchpoints, which exist to satisfy card-network trial rules — build the equivalent yourself on Razorpay):
    1. Welcome message + receipt immediately on signup, even for the ₹1 charge, restating all terms.
    2. Reminder at least 3 days before the first full charge, naming the amount and date, with a one-tap cancel link.
    3. Your own pre-debit WhatsApp 2–3 hours before the bank's mandatory 24-hour notice, every cycle.
    4. Receipt after every successful charge, including grievance-redressal contact.
    
    All of the above in Hindi, Hinglish and English at minimum, selected by the language the user chose during the quiz — the audience is explicitly described as not fluent in English, and a mandate authorised on a screen the user could not read is both a conversion loss and a chargeback waiting to happen.

**Risks**

- Clause (iii) of 'Subscription trap' is broadly worded and untested on this exact fact pattern. A ₹1 introductory period is a defensible reading — the clause bites on a 'free subscription' — but if the marketing copy anywhere calls it free, trial, 'try it free', 'मुफ्त', or shows ₹0, the defence collapses. Get a written opinion from an Indian consumer-law counsel on the specific paywall screens before launch; this is a few lakh rupees of insurance against a ₹10–50 lakh statutory exposure plus takedown risk.
- Razorpay's cross-border Import Flow recurring support is documented but commercially opaque: pricing is case-by-case, and AED settlement appears in the docs page but is absent from Razorpay's own product blog list (which names USD, SGD, AUD, CAD, EUR, GBP, HKD, INR, MYR). Do not architect the UAE bridge until AED settlement, UPI AutoPay enablement for import flow, and actual rates are confirmed in writing.
- Astrology is a globally high-risk acquiring category. Razorpay is demonstrably friendly today (AstroTalk), but acquirer policy can change, and Paddle's explicit pseudo-science ban plus Stripe's psychic/fortune-telling prohibitions in Japan, Mexico and Thailand show which way industry sentiment points. Keep Cashfree or PayU integrated and pre-approved as a hot standby; a single-processor dependency on a restricted-adjacent category is an existential single point of failure.
- The ₹15,000 UPI Autopay ceiling caps any annual plan sold on UPI. An annual plan priced above ₹15,000 must go on cards, which excludes the large majority of the target audience. Keep every annual SKU well under ₹15,000 — this constrains the maximum realistic annual ARPU on the primary rail.
- Mandate maximums cannot be raised without full re-authorisation by every existing subscriber; the renewal simply fails and the subscription goes past_due. If the mandate is registered at the launch price, any future price increase forces a re-consent campaign across the entire base with predictable heavy attrition. Register mandates with headroom from day one.
- OIDAR GST exposure is live from the first Indian customer if the UAE entity keeps collecting. Registration is mandatory regardless of turnover, 18% IGST applies, an Indian representative must be appointed and GSTR-5A filed monthly — and the Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules apply extra-territorially to entities systematically serving Indian consumers, so the offshore structure provides no shelter from either tax or consumer law while adding compliance overhead.
- Regulatory tightening is in progress. RBI's draft dark-pattern rules for commercial banks (issued 11 February 2026, proposed effective 1 July 2026) will likely make the pre-debit opt-out even more prominent in bank apps and messages, mechanically increasing monthly cancellation rates for every subscription business in India. Any financial model should stress-test a step-change increase in voluntary churn in H2 2026.
- Consumer priming is hostile: 67% of Indian app/SaaS buyers report having been caught in a subscription trap and 71% report hidden charges (LocalCircles, 44,000+ responses, 331 districts). A 30–60 year-old, non-English-fluent user being asked for a UPI Autopay mandate at the end of a quiz is exactly the population that has been burned. If the mandate ask is not framed with total transparency in their own language, the conversion loss will be blamed on price when the real cause is distrust.
- Several operational numbers used here — UPI Autopay 8–15% cycle failure, 85%→70% success decay over six months, 92–95% Intent flow success, ~30% WhatsApp dunning recovery — come from processor and practitioner blogs (Razorpay, vmobify, BigHelpers), not NPCI primary data. They are directionally consistent across independent sources but should be treated as planning estimates, and replaced with your own cohort data within the first 90 days.
- The full text of the RBI Digital Payments – E-mandate Framework, 2026 was read through secondary legal analyses (IndiaLaw, KPMG, SCC Times, Conventus, Bar & Bench) because the RBI Master Directions index did not surface it under that title. Before finalising the compliance build, have counsel pull the primary circular (RBI/DPSS/2026-27/396, 21 April 2026) and verify the AFA-free ceiling and notification obligations against the original text.

### design-language-nonai

**Scope:** Design-language research for VedicHour's pivot to a subscription product for a mainstream Indian audience (30-60, mobile, many not fluent in English). Three questions: (a) what concretely separates crafted consumer design from AI-generated/template design, and what a distinctive, premium, culturally-rooted Vedic-astrology visual direction looks like — with real hex values, type pairings and illustration direction; (b) vernacular/multilingual UX for Hindi/Hinglish/regional, including Devanagari type mechanics and how Indian apps handle language; (c) copy guidelines that read as human-written. Method: (1) read the current published catalogues of "AI slop" design tells; (2) rather than rely on secondary write-ups, I pulled the live front-ends of the named comparison products and extracted their actual self-hosted font files and CSS colour tokens as primary evidence of their type and palette decisions; (3) researched Devanagari typesetting mechanics, Indian-language internet usage, and Indian recurring-payment rails; (4) audited VedicHour's own codebase (tailwind.config.ts, globals.css, layout.tsx, public/fonts, package.json) against every tell found. Not covered: pricing strategy, SEO keyword work, growth channels, or the quiz's question logic beyond its visual/copy structure.

**Sources studied:** Impeccable — "Slop" (impeccable.style/slop/): catalogue of 66 named AI-design patterns across visual detail, colour, typography, layout, copy and motion, Developers Digest — "AI Design Slop: 16 Patterns That Out Your App as Vibe-Coded" (developersdigest.tech/blog/ai-design-slop-and-how-to-spot-it), 925 Studios — "AI Slop Fonts and Gradients: The Tells That Give Away AI Design" and "AI Slop Web Design: Complete Guide" (925studios.co), prg.sh — "Why Your AI Keeps Building the Same Purple Gradient Website" (distributional convergence explanation), SmoothUI — "AI Design Slop: Why AI-Generated UI Looks Generic" (smoothui.dev/blog/ai-design-slop), PRIMARY: live front-end extraction (curl + grep of self-hosted font files and CSS custom properties) from ouraring.com, superhuman.com, linear.app, notion.com, headspace.com, groww.in, cred.club, whoop.com, simple.life — September 2026, Dezeen / Creative Review / It's Nice That / AIGA Eye on Design on Airbnb Cereal by Dalton Maag, Creative Bloq / Monotype / Johnson Banks on Duolingo's Feather Bold (Fontsmith, Krista Radoeva, 2019), KPMG–Google, "Indian Languages — Defining India's Internet" (kpmg.com), via Slator, Social Beat and Inc42 summaries, Nature Humanities & Social Sciences Communications — "Social, economic, and demographic factors drive the emergence of Hinglish code-mixing on social media" (s41599-024-03058-6), Process9 — "Why Indian AMC Apps Lose Tier 2 and Tier 3 Investors" (B30 localization analysis), PhonePe Design blog — "Building a seamless payment experience for millions of Indians" (phonepe.com/blog/design/), Alphabettes — Pooja Saxena, "Devanagari Typography 101: A guide for typesetting with Latin", TypeTogether — "Devanagari Type Anatomy" by Pooja Saxena (shirorekha, matras, vertical metrics), Google Fonts Guide — "Vertical Metrics" (googlefonts.github.io/gf-guide/metrics.html), Google Design — "Google Fonts Embrace India's Diverse Languages" (Ek Type, Mukta, Girish Dalvi), Indian Type Foundry — Kohinoor Devanagari (Satya Rajpurohit); Ek Type — Mukta, Anek Devanagari, Smartling — "Website language selectors: Best practices"; NN/g — "Language Switchers on Ecommerce Sites: 6 Tips", Statcounter Global Stats — Mobile Screen Resolution Stats India, August 2026, A11Y Collective / Kittl / CSS-Tricks on WCAG minimum font size, contrast and line-height for older readers, RevenueCat — "State of Subscription Apps 2025" and "Inside Noom's Web-to-App Onboarding Funnel: UX Teardown", Razorpay — "UPI Autopay vs Card e-Mandates"; Equentis on UPI AutoPay's 53% share of recurring transactions; Chargebee/Stripe docs on the RBI e-mandate framework, Memeraki — "The Colours of the Earth: Traditional Pigments of India"; Wikipedia — Pahari painting; Amélie Conservation on Pahari border pigments, Wikipedia / Kalnirnay.com / Open The Magazine — Kalnirnay almanac circulation and the visual language of Indian mass-market calendars, SlopDetector — "Is the Em Dash an AI Tell? We Measured Dash Density Across Human vs AI Texts"; Plagiarism Today; ProofreaderPro on burstiness and perplexity, VedicHour codebase (primary): jyotish-ai/tailwind.config.ts, src/app/globals.css, src/app/layout.tsx, public/fonts/, package.json

**Findings**

- **"AI-generated look" is not a vibe — it is a published, enumerated checklist, and anyone in your target market's comment section can now recite it. Treat it as a defect list to diff against, not a matter of taste.**
  - Evidence: Impeccable's "Slop" catalogues 66 named patterns; Developers Digest names 16; 925 Studios and SmoothUI name overlapping sets. The consistently-named tells: a specific lavender "VibeCode Purple" and purple-to-blue gradients; glassmorphism/frosted cards; a thick colored border on one side of a rounded card (called "the single most recognizable tell"); identical feature cards with an icon on top; centered hero in a generic sans; a badge or tiny all-caps letter-spaced label directly above the H1; large colored glows and colored box-shadows; permanent dark mode with medium-grey body text; extreme border-radius (24px+); numbered 1-2-3 step rows; big-number stat banners; em-dash-heavy copy; bounce/elastic easing and pulsing status dots. prg.sh explains the mechanism: models return the statistical median of their training data, so "normal is what a model produces when left to its own defaults."
  - So what: You can audit against this mechanically before a designer ever opens the file. Any pattern on the list that survives in your UI is a liability regardless of how good it looks in isolation, because the reference class it now belongs to is 'someone vibe-coded this in an afternoon' — which is fatal when you are asking a 45-year-old to hand over a recurring mandate.
- **The strongest single craft signal among top-tier subscription products is not layout or colour — it is that they pay for their typeface. Almost every product in the comparison set ships a proprietary, commissioned, or exclusively-licensed family, self-hosted.**
  - Evidence: Extracted directly from their live front-ends in September 2026: Superhuman self-hosts SuperSans-VF, SuperSerif-VF and SuperSansMono-VF (a complete bespoke family with upright and italic variable cuts). Oura ships AkkuratLL-Light/Regular (Lineto) plus PPEditorialNew-Light/Ultralight/UltralightItalic (Pangram Pangram). Headspace ships HeadspaceApercu in eight cuts plus Apercu Pro Mono — a custom-named cut of Colophon's Apercu. Groww ships GrowwSans-Variable alongside Söhne (Klim). CRED ships Denton in ten cuts (Regular/Medium/Light/Bold/ExtraBold plus italics), Gilroy, and a display face called BetterGrade. Notion ships NotionInter-Regular/Medium/Bold — its own cut. Airbnb commissioned Cereal from Dalton Maag in six weights, starting from the shape of its own logo; Duolingo commissioned Feather Bold from Fontsmith (designer Krista Radoeva) via Johnson Banks, derived from the owl mascot's wing.
  - So what: Inter is not the tell — Linear self-hosts InterVariable and reads as one of the most crafted products on the web. The tell is the *default stack*: Inter (or Cormorant + DM Sans) pulled unmodified from Google Fonts and used exactly as every template uses it. For VedicHour the leverage is doubled, because a licensed multiscript family solves the brand problem and the Hindi problem with one purchase.
- **VedicHour's current design is simultaneously the astrology-app cliché AND trips at least four named AI-slop tells. It is the exact aesthetic the brief says to avoid.**
  - Evidence: From jyotish-ai/tailwind.config.ts and src/app/globals.css: the default canvas is deep plum-indigo (--color-bg #0A0713, surface #120C1E, surface-2 #1B1329, border #2A1E3D) with a gold accent (--amber #D4A853, --amber-glow #FCD34D) and an indigo secondary (#9AA0F2). Fonts in src/app/layout.tsx are Cormorant Garamond (display) + DM Sans (body) + JetBrains Mono — three free Google families, and one of the most-used free display/body pairings in existence. Grep across src/: 44 occurrences of bg-gradient-to / shadow-glow / rounded-3xl across 33 files; backdrop-blur (glassmorphism) in 9+ components including Navbar, ReportTabs, GeneratingScreen and ExitIntentUpsell; a named `shadow-glow-amber` utility. Dark mode is the default with the light "parchment" theme behind [data-theme="light"]. Against the tell lists this is: permanent dark mode (#5), gradient backgrounds (#7), colored glows/box-shadows (#8), glassmorphism, extreme border-radius. Separately, industry write-ups describe the astrology-category default as "mysticism meets minimalism in a dark-themed interface... deep purple and gradient blue... celestial icons, glyphs."
  - So what: There is genuine craft in the file — the 2.8%-opacity inline-SVG film grain on body::after is a real, deliberate anti-flatness move, and the code comments show someone thinking hard about why cards read as floating text. But the top-level decisions (dark plum + gold + Cormorant + glow + gradient) are the two most-copied templates on the internet stacked on each other. This is fixable at the token layer without rebuilding components — the config already routes everything through named tokens.
- **The codebase has zero capacity to render Hindi in brand voice. Not 'partial' — zero. This is the single largest gap between the product and its stated market.**
  - Evidence: public/fonts/ contains exactly six files, all Latin-only subsets: cormorant-garamond-latin-400/600, dm-sans-latin-300/400/500, jetbrains-mono-latin-400. package.json contains no i18n library (no next-intl, react-i18next, i18next or next-translate). No file under src/ references "devanagari". Cormorant Garamond has no Devanagari coverage at all, so any Hindi headline falls back to whatever the OS supplies — Noto Sans Devanagari on Android, Nirmala UI on Windows — meaning the brand's voice disappears at precisely the moment the target user starts reading.
  - So what: Hindi cannot be 'added later' as a translation layer on this foundation. Font loading, the type scale, line-height, and every fixed-height container have to be rebuilt for a script with different vertical metrics. Doing it at the token layer now costs days; doing it after 200 components exist costs months.
- **Vernacular is not a feature for this audience — it is the market. And the Indian companies that won this segment treated it as an infrastructure decision, not a translation project.**
  - Evidence: KPMG–Google projected over 536 million non-English-speaking Indian internet users by 2025, found ~90% of new internet users prefer regional-language content, ~75% of new users prefer their native language, and 70% of Indians find vernacular digital content more reliable than English — with Hindi the largest single share at roughly 50%. Groww: over 60% of users now come from Tier 2/3 cities, attributed to localized onboarding and vernacular content, with vernacular-targeted regions showing 25-40% higher conversion. PhonePe ships in 11 Indian languages, localizes the app, the help section AND chat support, notes ~80% of its users are from Tier 2/3 and beyond, and deliberately leaned on imagery over text for first-time internet users. Process9's analysis of Indian mutual-fund apps is blunt: "A translated homepage sitting on top of an English-only journey is not localization. It is decoration."
  - So what: Half-localizing is worse than not localizing, because it teaches the user that the app will switch back to English at the moment of highest stakes (payment, error, support). The commitment is the whole chain — quiz, paywall, daily card, push notification, payment receipt, cancellation flow, support reply — or nothing.
- **Hindi and Hinglish are two different products for two halves of your stated 30-60 audience, and picking one loses the other.**
  - Evidence: The Nature HSSC study of Hinglish code-mixing found the proportion of users preferring code-mixed Hindi-English rose from 44.9% in 2014 to 56.3% after 2020 (~1.2% annual growth), while noting Devanagari-script usage on the same platform grew from 35% in 2014 to 82% in 2022. Secondary analysis of the same trend places Hinglish as strongest for metro audiences (Delhi NCR, Mumbai, Bangalore, Pune) and users aged 18-34, while pure Hindi in Devanagari performs best for Tier 2/3, rural audiences and older demographics.
  - So what: VedicHour's 30-60 band straddles the split exactly. A 34-year-old in Gurgaon arriving from ChatGPT and a 56-year-old in Kanpur arriving from a Google search for 'rahu kaal aaj' want the same maths in two different registers. Three modes — English, हिंदी (Devanagari), and Hinglish (Roman) — is the minimum, and Hinglish must be hand-written, never machine-transliterated from the Hindi.
- **Devanagari breaks Latin-derived design systems mechanically, not stylistically. Line-height, vertical metrics and fixed-height containers are where it fails.**
  - Evidence: Pooja Saxena (TypeTogether, Alphabettes): Devanagari has no x-height — letters are measured between the shirorekha (headline) and baseline, and matras attach above and below, sometimes joining above the shirorekha or below the baseline. Optical alignment should match Latin's x-height to the shirorekha height, not the baseline. "Devanagari may require additional line spacing as compared to the Latin script." All-caps Latin set alongside needs an increased point size and a baseline shift. Forcing Latin tracking onto Devanagari breaks the shirorekha. The Google Fonts vertical-metrics guide states the standard rule (metric sum 120-130% of UPM) "can often be too tight if your font covers more languages than basic Latin" and that for Devanagari "it may make more sense for the sum of the metrics to exceed 130% to avoid interline glyph collisions"; Use_Typo_Metrics must be enabled so Windows uses Typo rather than Win values. Localization guidance separately warns that Hindi's ascending and descending marks clip when a UI enforces strict vertical limits, "cutting off essential diacritical marks, which can alter the meaning of the text."
  - So what: Every fixed-height button, single-line card title, truncated list row and `overflow: hidden` container in the current build is a future Hindi bug. The fix is cheap if made a system rule now (min-height + padding, per-language line-height, no uppercase, no letter-spacing on Devanagari) and expensive as a retrofit.
- **Your audience's eyes and their phones both demand a bigger, calmer type scale than a design-led startup's default — and India's dominant viewport is narrower than most design files.**
  - Evidence: Statcounter (India, mobile, August 2026): 360x800 is the single most common resolution at 18.46%, ahead of 393x873 at 8.44%, 360x804 at 4.66% and 393x876 at 4.02%. Accessibility guidance: treat 14pt as the absolute floor, target 16-17 generally, and 18-20px as the default for audiences 55+, because presbyopia affects effectively everyone over 50. WCAG requires 4.5:1 contrast for normal text, 3:1 for large text (18.66px bold or 24px regular) and non-text elements, and a line-height of at least 1.5× the font size for body text.
  - So what: Design and QA at a 360px logical width with 17-18px body as the baseline, not 16px at 390px. Combined with Hindi's need for ~1.7 line-height and ~30% string expansion, the practical consequence is that you fit far less on screen than a Western wellness app — which forces the editorial discipline the brief is asking for anyway.
- **The card-required free trial is a design constraint the Indian payment rail will not support at scale. UPI Autopay is the mass-market recurring instrument, and it changes the trial screen's copy and flow.**
  - Evidence: UPI AutoPay went from 33% of all recurring payments in January 2024 to over 53% by January 2025, with mandates crossing 1.27 billion in November 2025 (~10x in under two years) and over 120 million new mandates created monthly. Cards' share of recurring payments fell from 42% to 31% over the same period. Credit-card penetration in India is around 8%. RevenueCat's analysis states it plainly: "in India, where card penetration is only around 8%, the card wall does not filter for intent; it blocks the trial start outright." The RBI e-mandate framework requires AFA at one-time registration, a pre-debit notification at least 24 hours before each charge with merchant name, amount, date and time, an opt-out for that specific debit, and the ability to withdraw the mandate at any time; the AFA-free per-transaction ceiling is ₹15,000.
  - So what: At ₹1,999/month you sit comfortably under the ₹15,000 AFA ceiling, so post-registration debits are frictionless — but only on a rail the user actually has. Making UPI Autopay the default mandate and card the secondary option is a design decision with a bigger conversion effect than anything on the paywall screen. The mandatory 24-hour bank SMS also means the user WILL be told about the charge; your own reminder should arrive first, or the bank's message becomes the surprise.
- **Noom's quiz is the right pattern and the wrong length for this audience. Its power is sunk cost plus playback of the user's own words — both of which survive compression; its 113 screens do not.**
  - Evidence: RevenueCat's teardown: Noom's web-to-app funnel runs up to 113 screens and 10-15 minutes; each step collects first-party data and increases psychological commitment through sunk cost; the paywall headline reads "Your personalized health plan is ready" and references the specific goal weight the user typed earlier. RevenueCat's State of Subscription Apps 2025: hard paywalls achieve a 12.1% median download-to-paid conversion versus 2.2% for freemium; 82% of trial starts occur the same day as install; trials of 17-32 days convert to paid at 45.7% versus shorter trials' lower rates; median trial-to-paid is 39.9% for Health & Fitness with the top decile around 68.3%.
  - So what: Keep the mechanic (progressive investment, then play their exact words back at the paywall), cut the length to roughly 18-24 screens / 3-4 minutes, and pick a 14-day trial rather than 7 — the benchmark data favours the longer window. Also: the brief's 60%+ paywall conversion target sits at the top decile of the best-performing categories, so it should be a stretch goal, not the plan.
- **There is a culturally-native design reference for a timing product that is far more trusted — and far less clichéd — than the cosmos: the printed panchang almanac.**
  - Evidence: Kalnirnay, founded 1973 as a hand-printed Marathi almanac, is recorded by the Audit Bureau of Circulation (India) at 18,187,168 copies, described as the world's largest-selling publication, with around 19 million copies sold annually and over 35 regional variants; its content is exactly panchang, auspicious days, festivals, sunrise/sunset and monthly bhavishya. Traditional Indian calendar design is characterised by "looming, bold typography and highly saturated colours." Its app ships in Marathi, Hindi, English and Gujarati.
  - So what: Your 30-60 Indian user has an existing, deeply-familiar mental model for 'authoritative timing information': a dense, tabular, red-and-ink printed page that their parents had on the kitchen wall. Building on that grammar — printed rules, tabular data, a masthead, festival markers, today's row tinted — reads as credible and rooted, and is a design space no competitor and no AI default occupies. AstroSage is criticised for a UI that "feels outdated, cluttered with ads"; the opportunity is the almanac's authority without its clutter.
- **Indian painting traditions supply a real, defensible, documented palette — which is the honest answer to "a distinctive restrained palette" rather than picking pretty hexes.**
  - Evidence: Pahari and Basohli miniature painting used a documented pigment set: lead white, vermilion (cinnabar), verdigris, azurite, lapis lazuli, indigo, Indian yellow, orpiment, malachite, carmine and lampblack, bound in gum arabic, applied in flat opaque layers and burnished with agate or conch shell. Basohli specifically is characterised by bold outlines and glowing primary reds and yellows; Pahari borders were painted in indigo.
  - So what: This gives every colour in the system a provenance and a job, which is exactly what a generated palette lacks. It is also structurally anti-slop: these are flat opaque fields with black outlines and no gradients or volumetric shading, so committing to the reference automatically eliminates the gradient/glow/glassmorphism family of tells.
- **The copy tells are measurable, and the highest-leverage fix is sentence-length variation — not word substitution.**
  - Evidence: A controlled measurement puts GPT-4.1 at 10.62 em dashes per 1,000 words against a matched human baseline of 3.23 per 1,000 (~3.3x). Tracking of PubMed abstracts documents sharp post-2022 rises in "delve," "meticulous," "underscore," "boast" and "intricate." Craft guidance identifies burstiness — deliberate variation in sentence length — as "the single highest-leverage craft fix and the one detectors weight most heavily," because AI prose tends toward uniform medium-length sentences, and notes that "concrete, first-hand detail is what readers trust and what classifiers find least predictable; one real specific per paragraph changes the whole feel." The same sources caution that every one of these patterns also appears in human writing and any of them can be removed with a single instruction — they are a reason to look closer, never proof.
  - So what: Don't chase a detector score; chase the underlying property. An astrology product has an unfair advantage here: real times, real Sanskrit terms, real dates and the user's own birth data are inherently unpredictable, specific tokens. Copy that says "Rahu kaal today runs 10:42-12:06" is both more useful and structurally less AI-like than copy that says "navigate your day with cosmic clarity."
- **Language switcher mechanics have settled best practice that most Indian products still get wrong — and getting it right is a visible trust signal to exactly this audience.**
  - Evidence: Consolidated guidance (Smartling, NN/g): place the selector in a highly visible spot — top corner or main navigation, consistent across all pages, in the hamburger on mobile; always display options as endonyms in their native script ("Español", not "Spanish"); never use flags, because flags represent countries not languages and one flag cannot represent a language spoken across many; never let geographic auto-detection override without a working manual selector. PhonePe applies the localized-everything version: app, help section and chat support all language-selectable, with the observed result that "more and more customers opt to use the app in a language they are comfortable with."
  - So what: A first-run, full-screen language picker before the quiz begins (rather than a setting buried three levels deep) is the pattern Indian mass-market apps use, and it also tells you which of the three copy registers to write the entire funnel in — including the paywall.

**Apply to VedicHour**

- **Do a mechanical anti-slop pass at the token layer first — before any redesign. This is a days-long change, not a rebuild, because tailwind.config.ts already routes everything through named tokens.**
  - Concrete removals, all verifiable by grep in jyotish-ai/src: (1) Delete the `shadow-glow-*` utilities and all `shadow-glow-amber` usages — replace emphasis with a 1px printed rule in --rule and a 2px vermilion left-edge marker used ONLY on the single 'act now' row, never on a card. (2) Remove `backdrop-blur` from all 9+ components (Navbar, ReportTabs, GeneratingScreen, ExitIntentUpsell, RightNowCard, FeedbackWidget, LiveTelemetry, SynastryResultDisplay) — use an opaque surface token instead; a nav that goes opaque on scroll reads as more considered than a frosted one. (3) Audit the 44 `bg-gradient-to` / `rounded-3xl` occurrences across 33 files: allow zero decorative gradients (a gradient may only encode data, e.g. a muhurta-strength band along a timeline), and cap border-radius at 6px for cards / 8px for buttons / 2px for chips — the 24px+ blob radius is a named tell. (4) Flip the default theme from NIGHT to PAPER: `:root` becomes the light almanac palette, dark mode moves behind `[data-theme="dark"]` and `prefers-color-scheme`. Permanent dark mode is a named tell, and it is also wrong for a 50-year-old reading long-form guidance on a phone in Indian daylight. (5) Keep the body::after film grain — it is the one genuinely crafted detail in the current build; retune to 0.035 opacity on the paper canvas. (6) Ban from the design system: centered hero + three icon-top feature cards, all-caps letter-spaced eyebrow labels, badges directly above an H1, numbered 1-2-3 step rows, big-number stat banners, pulsing dots, and any card with a coloured border on one side used decoratively.
- **Adopt the "Almanac" visual direction: a printed panchang page rendered with the pigment palette of Pahari miniature painting. Light-first, warm, tabular, high-contrast, zero cosmos.**
  - Replace the plum-indigo/gold tokens in tailwind.config.ts and globals.css with these. LIGHT (default, `:root`) — surfaces: --paper #F7F1E4 (unbleached almanac stock; not #FFFFFF, not the template's #FAF9F6), --paper-raised #FDF9F0, --paper-inset #EFE6D3, --rule #DCCFB4 (1px printed rule — this replaces box-shadows as the primary separator), --tint-today #F2E2B6 (turmeric wash, for the selected/today row only). Ink: --ink #221A12 (lampblack, warm — 15.2:1 on paper), --ink-2 #5C4E3C (7.2:1), --ink-3 #7A6A52 (4.69:1, the true 'faint but still AA' token — do NOT use a lighter grey for small text). Pigments, each with exactly one job: --vermilion #C1352B (4.93:1 — 'act now', muhurta open, masthead rule; the panchang red), --indigo #253C6A (9.67:1 — primary structure, headers, chart strokes, links; this is the brand colour and it is deliberately nothing like #6366F1), --lapis #1B4F8C (secondary data series), --orpiment #E0A320 (1.98:1 on paper — FILL AND MARKER ONLY, never text on paper; use for festival dots, highlight bands, the kundli house fill), --malachite #2E6B4F (5.66:1 — favourable/auspicious state), --carmine #8C2F39 (7.23:1 — caution/avoid state). DARK (opt-in, 'lamp-lit almanac', warm not plum): --bg #17120C, --surface #1F1912, --inset #2A2219, --rule #3A3025, --ink #F2E8D6, --ink-2 #A2937C; pigments lift to --vermilion #E0574A, --indigo #8FA6D8, --orpiment #F0BF52, --malachite #58A87C, --carmine #C4616A. System rules: no gradient may be decorative; no coloured glow exists; separation is achieved with 1px rules and flat fills, exactly as flat opaque pigment layers work in the source tradition.
- **Buy one multiscript type family that covers Latin and Devanagari together, and retire Cormorant Garamond entirely. This solves the 'looks AI-generated' problem and the 'has no Hindi' problem with one purchase.**
  - PAID PATH (recommended, ~the cost of two weeks of ads): license Kohinoor Devanagari + Kohinoor Latin from Indian Type Foundry (Satya Rajpurohit; the family is in the Design Museum London's permanent collection since 2016). One family, both scripts, drawn together, low-contrast, works at both display and text sizes, and — critically — it is a paid license, so it will not appear in the next template. FREE PATH (if budget is zero, and still not a default): Anek Devanagari + Anek Latin (ITF, OFL, variable on weight AND width axes) for display, plus Mukta (Ek Type, seven weights, covers Devanagari + Gujarati + Gurmukhi + Tamil + Latin) for body — Mukta is used by Dainik Jagran and Lokmat, so it carries newspaper familiarity for a 30-60 Hindi reader, which is precisely the register you want. Concrete changes to src/app/layout.tsx and public/fonts/: delete cormorant-garamond-latin-*.woff2 (it has zero Devanagari coverage, so every Hindi headline currently loses the brand voice); delete jetbrains-mono (a third family is unnecessary — get tabular figures from the body family with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`, which you need anyway for panchang tables); add devanagari subsets with their own @font-face and `unicode-range: U+0900-097F, U+A8E0-A8FF, U+1CD0-1CFF` so English users never download them; `font-display: swap` on all. Before shipping, verify the chosen font has Use_Typo_Metrics enabled and a vertical-metric sum above 130% of UPM, or conjuncts and matras will clip on Windows.
- **Commission a small, finite illustration system drawn from Indian miniature painting and the north-Indian kundli diagram. Do not generate images, and do not license stock celestial art.**
  - Build the entire graphic language from the north-Indian kundli chart (the square with diagonals) — it is a shape no Western astrology app owns and it is instantly legible to your audience. Icon system: 2px stroke on a 24px grid, ink-only, geometric, derived from that square-and-diamond grammar plus the planetary glyphs; no emoji, ever, and no stock geometric icon set. Illustration: commission ~24 flat-fill vignettes in the Pahari/Basohli manner — flat opaque colour fields, a firm black outline, a decorative 3px vermilion border rule, no gradients, no volumetric shading, no perspective. One per life domain (career, marriage, money, health, property, litigation, children, travel), one per dasha lord, plus a handful of ritual moments (the quiz completion, the report cover, the trial-start confirmation). Photography, if used at all: documentary images of Indian domestic life — a wall calendar beside a switchboard, a diary on a kitchen table, hands holding a phone in a shop — shot or licensed from Indian photographers, never global stock. HARD BANS: purple or blue nebula backgrounds, 3D glassy planets, glowing orbs, tarot cards, crystal balls, mandalas used as generic wallpaper, the 'cosmic woman silhouette', lens flares, and any image with the smooth plastic sheen of a generative model. Budget note: 24 vignettes plus an icon set from a good Indian illustrator is a real but bounded cost, and it is the single most visible signal that a human made this.
- **Impose editorial asymmetry and a print-derived layout system, replacing the centered-hero-plus-card-grid structure.**
  - Grid: a 12-column grid where content sits on an asymmetric 7/5 or 8/4 split, not centered. Hero: left-aligned, text set against a wide left margin with the day's panchang data block sitting in the narrow right column — so the first screen shows real computed information (today's tithi, nakshatra, rahu kaal window) rather than a value proposition. Cards: no card grid of three identical icon-top boxes anywhere in the product. Where you would reach for cards, use an almanac table instead — rows separated by a 1px --rule, today's row tinted --tint-today, a 2px --vermilion left marker on the one row that matters now. Type scale (mobile-first at 360px logical width, which is India's #1 viewport at 18.46%): body 17px/1.55 Latin, headline-sm 20px, headline-md 24px, display 32-40px — resist a 5.5rem hero; it does not exist at 360px anyway. Section openers use a small-caps or medium-weight running head in --indigo with a rule beneath, in place of the all-caps letter-spaced eyebrow. One page = one dominant element; if two things on a screen are competing for the same emphasis, one is wrong.
- **Write a restrained motion spec with exactly one signature moment, and honour reduced-motion.**
  - Durations from Material 3 tokens: state changes and hovers 50-200ms (short); sheets, tab transitions and screen changes 250-400ms (medium); the one long transition 450-550ms. Easing: emphasized-decelerate on entry, standard on exit. BANNED: bounce/elastic/spring easing on dialogs and cards, auto-scrolling marquees, image scale-on-hover, pulsing status dots, infinite loops of any kind, and animated gradients. THE ONE SIGNATURE MOTION: a horizontal 'day band' — the 24-hour strip on the daily card — where the auspicious and inauspicious windows fill in left-to-right over ~500ms on first view each day, and the current-time marker slides. That is the product's single animated idea; everything else is instant or a 200ms crossfade. Wrap all of it in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { animation: none; transition-duration: 0.01ms }`.
- **Ship three language modes as a first-class architecture decision — English, हिंदी (Devanagari), Hinglish (Roman) — and localize the entire chain or nothing.**
  - Add next-intl (currently no i18n library exists in package.json) with locales `en`, `hi`, `hi-Latn` on path-prefixed routes (/hi/, /hi-latn/) with correct `hreflang` alternates for SEO. FIRST RUN: a full-screen language picker BEFORE the quiz's first question, three large tap targets, each labelled in its own script — `English` / `हिंदी` / `Hinglish` — with a one-line sample of real product copy underneath each so the choice is concrete. No flags, ever (a flag is a country, not a language). PERSISTENT: a language chip in the header on every page (not buried in settings), consistent position, showing the current language as an endonym. Persist to both a cookie and the Supabase user profile so it survives device changes. Never let IP geolocation override an explicit choice. THE CHAIN — every one of these must exist in the chosen language before you call it supported: quiz questions, quiz result playback, paywall, trial terms, payment mandate screen, daily card, push notifications, the report itself, email receipts, cancellation flow, error states, and support replies. Ship Hindi completely before adding a fourth language; Marathi, Bengali, Gujarati and Tamil come after Hindi is proven, and the Anek/Mukta choice above means the same type system already covers them.
- **Write the Devanagari typesetting rules into the design system now, as constraints on every component.**
  - Per-language CSS: `:lang(hi) { line-height: 1.7 }` for body against 1.55 for Latin; `:lang(hi) h1, :lang(hi) h2 { line-height: 1.3 }` against 1.15 Latin. Set Devanagari body at 1.05-1.10× the Latin size (Devanagari's optical size runs smaller because it has no x-height — align Latin's x-height to the shirorekha height, not the baseline). ABSOLUTE BANS in Hindi: `text-transform: uppercase` (meaningless in Devanagari and it destroys the reading line), `letter-spacing` on Devanagari runs (it breaks the shirorekha), fixed `height` on any text-bearing element (use `min-height` + padding), `overflow: hidden` on any text container, and single-line truncation on titles. Layout budget: assume +30% width for Hindi strings versus English, and up to +100% for short labels and single words — test every screen at 360px logical width with the longest Hindi string. Never translate the astrological vocabulary: keep muhurta/मुहूर्त, dasha/दशा, nakshatra/नक्षत्र, rahu kaal/राहु काल, tithi/तिथि, and gloss each once on first use. QA procedure, borrowed directly from the Process9 audit: switch the app to Hindi and walk the entire funnel end to end, counting every screen that breaks layout, silently falls back to English, or shows an unexplained English term. That count is your localization backlog.
- **Make UPI Autopay the default mandate rail and card the secondary option, and design the trial screen around the RBI e-mandate mechanics rather than around a Western card-trial pattern.**
  - Trial screen, in the user's chosen language, with these exact elements: (1) the amount and the date, stated plainly — 'आज ₹0. 20 सितंबर को ₹1,999. उससे पहले कभी भी बंद कर सकते हैं।' / 'Nothing today. ₹1,999 on 20 September. Cancel any time before that.' (2) UPI Autopay as the primary, largest button, with the user's UPI app icons shown; card as a secondary text link, not a second button. Rationale to hold the line on: UPI Autopay went from 33% of recurring payments in Jan 2024 to over 53% by Jan 2025 with 1.27bn mandates by Nov 2025 and 120M+ created monthly, while card penetration sits around 8% — a card wall in India does not filter for intent, it blocks the trial start. (3) At ₹1,999 you are under the ₹15,000 AFA ceiling, so no per-debit authentication is needed after registration — say so: 'One-time approval. No OTP each month.' (4) The bank is legally required to send a pre-debit notification at least 24 hours before each charge; send your own reminder 48 hours before so yours arrives first and the bank's SMS confirms rather than surprises. (5) A visible, one-tap cancel path in-app, and say where it is on the trial screen itself. Trial length: 14 days, not 7 — RevenueCat's 2025 data shows 17-32 day trials converting to paid at 45.7%, materially above short trials.
- **Compress the Noom pattern to ~20 screens and make the pre-paywall proof a real computed output, not a promise.**
  - Structure, one question per screen, thumb-reachable answers at the bottom, a thin --indigo progress rule at the top (not a percentage, not a 1-2-3 stepper): (1) Language picker. (2) Name + 'What is on your mind right now?' — six real Indian life-decision categories with the miniature vignettes: करियर / नौकरी, शादी / रिश्ता, पैसा / कर्ज़, सेहत, ज़मीन-जायदाद, बच्चे. Not 'self-growth'. (3) Birth date, birth time, birth place — place as an Indian city+district autocomplete; time with an honest 'अगर ठीक याद नहीं' option offering morning/afternoon/evening/night plus a one-line note that you will flag the resulting uncertainty rather than hide it. (4) Six to eight adaptive questions that branch on the chosen concern, each written in the register of a family astrologer asking, not a form collecting. (5) A playback screen every fifth question that repeats their own words back: 'राजेश — करियर, और अगले छह महीने में फैसला लेना है। ठीक है।' (6) TWO FREE REAL OUTPUTS BEFORE THE PAYWALL: today's rahu kaal computed from their actual coordinates, and one true statement about their current mahadasha with the dates. This is the trust move that a mainstream Indian user needs and that Noom does not have to make — you can prove the maths is real before asking for money. (7) Paywall headline built from their own inputs: 'राजेश, आपके करियर वाले सवाल का 90-दिन का समय-नक्शा तैयार है।' Target 3-4 minutes total. Set the expectation that 60%+ paywall conversion is top-decile (RevenueCat's Health & Fitness median trial-to-paid is 39.9%, top decile ~68.3%) and instrument for 30-45%.
- **Adopt a written copy standard with hard numeric limits and worked examples in all three registers.**
  - DON'T — banned outright, with the replacement: 'Unlock your cosmic blueprint' → 'Your chart, calculated from your birth time.' | 'Delve into the tapestry of your destiny' → delete; 'delve', 'tapestry', 'meticulous', 'underscore', 'intricate', 'harness', 'elevate', 'empower', 'streamline', 'supercharge', 'seamless', 'journey', 'in today's fast-paced world' are all banned strings — put them in a lint rule. | 'It's not just an astrology app — it's a daily companion' → banned construction ('not just X, it's Y'); say what it does. | Em dashes capped at 3 per 1,000 words (measured GPT-4.1 density is 10.62/1,000 against a human baseline of 3.23/1,000). | No tricolon headlines ('Clarity, confidence, and calm'). | No sentence in a paragraph may be within ±3 words of the length of the one before it. DO — with real examples: (1) Burstiness. Short. Then a longer sentence that carries the actual detail, the caveat, and the number. Then short again. (2) One concrete specific per paragraph — a real time, a real date, a real term: EN 'Your rahu kaal today runs 10:42 to 12:06. Don't sign anything in it.' HI 'आज राहु काल 10:42 से 12:06 तक है। इस बीच कोई कागज़ मत साइन कीजिए।' HINGLISH 'Aaj rahu kaal 10:42 se 12:06 tak hai. Is beech koi paper sign mat kijiye.' (3) Name the method — it is both honest and unpredictable text: 'Lahiri ayanamsha. Vimshottari dasha. Calculated from 04:12, Kanpur.' (4) Hedge where the tradition hedges: 'Classical sources disagree here. We follow Parashara, and we tell you when we've made a choice.' (5) Use their inputs verbatim. (6) HINDI IS WRITTEN, NOT TRANSLATED — hire a Hindi copywriter; the register is a respected family astrologer speaking to an adult, not the Sanskritised officialese of a government form. Avoid शुद्ध हिंदी coinages for common English words (say 'रिपोर्ट', not 'प्रतिवेदन'). (7) HINGLISH IS A THIRD ORIGINAL — hand-written Roman-script Hindi with natural code-mixing, never a machine transliteration of the Hindi file. (8) The test that beats every detector: read the string aloud. If you would not say it in that form to a 52-year-old in Kanpur, cut it.
- **Set accessibility and device floors as build-blocking rules, given a 30-60 audience on entry-level Android.**
  - Body text 17px minimum, 18px for report long-form (presbyopia affects effectively everyone over 50; guidance is 18-20px default for 55+; 14px is the absolute floor and you should never be near it). Line-height 1.55 Latin / 1.7 Devanagari — WCAG requires at least 1.5×. Contrast: 4.5:1 for all body text, 3:1 for large text and every non-text control; the palette above is pre-verified — --ink 15.2:1, --ink-2 7.2:1, --ink-3 4.69:1, --vermilion 4.93:1, --malachite 5.66:1, --carmine 7.23:1, --indigo 9.67:1, all on --paper; --orpiment is 1.98:1 and is therefore a fill only, never text. Touch targets 48×48px minimum, primary actions in the bottom third of the screen (top corners are unreachable one-handed). Design and QA at 360×800 first — it is 18.46% of Indian mobile traffic, the single largest bucket. Performance: self-host all fonts with unicode-range subsetting so a Hindi user downloads Devanagari and an English user does not; total font payload under 150KB per locale; no web font blocks first paint.

**Risks**

- The 60%+ paywall conversion target is top-decile, not baseline. RevenueCat's 2025 data puts median trial-to-paid at 39.9% for Health & Fitness with the top decile around 68.3%, and hard-paywall download-to-paid at a 12.1% median. Building the business model on 60% will produce a false read on whether the redesign worked. Instrument for 30-45% and treat anything above as upside.
- A card-required trial in India is a conversion wall, not a quality filter. With ~8% credit-card penetration and UPI Autopay now above 53% of recurring payments, requiring a card blocks trial starts among exactly the mainstream 30-60 users the pivot targets. If the payment integration cannot support UPI Autopay at launch, the trial design should change before the visual design does.
- Half-finished craft is worse than clean restraint. Commissioning a type license plus 24 illustrations plus a Hindi copywriter is a real budget and a real timeline; a product with three bespoke illustrations and a generated palette everywhere else reads as more incoherent than a disciplined, plain one. If the budget only covers one thing, buy the multiscript typeface — it carries the most signal per rupee and it solves the Hindi problem at the same time.
- Localization multiplies QA surface faster than it multiplies revenue. Every language is a full chain — quiz, paywall, mandate, notification, report, receipt, cancellation, support. Process9's finding is that a translated homepage on an English journey is decoration; shipping four languages badly will damage trust more than shipping Hindi alone very well.
- Cultural symbolism carries political and religious charge that a design system cannot neutralise. Saffron in particular, and overt deity or sect-specific iconography generally, will read as an affiliation statement to some portion of a pan-Indian audience. The almanac/panchang direction is deliberately the safer register — it is civic and domestic rather than devotional — but every illustration should be reviewed by Indian reviewers from more than one region and community before it ships.
- AI-detector scores are an unreliable target. The same sources that catalogue the tells note that every pattern appears in human writing and any of them can be stripped with one instruction — they are a reason to look closer, never proof. Optimising copy to beat a detector will produce strange prose; optimise for a human reading it aloud instead, and use the detector only as a smoke alarm.
- I could not verify current advertising-platform policy on astrology claims (Google Ads and Meta both restrict certain categories of divination and outcome claims in some markets). Before writing any acquisition copy that promises outcomes — career, marriage, health, money — get the policy checked, because it constrains headline copy on the very pages this design work will produce.
- The visual direction proposed here is deliberately unlike the category. That is the point, but it carries a real risk: a mainstream Indian user searching for 'rahu kaal' may not immediately recognise a warm printed almanac as an astrology product the way they recognise a dark purple starfield. Mitigate by making the north-Indian kundli square and the panchang table visible above the fold on landing pages, and A/B the hero against the current dark treatment rather than assuming the redesign wins.

### retention-daily-loop

**Scope:** Research brief on daily-engagement and retention mechanics for VedicHour's pivot to a recurring subscription. Covered: Duolingo (streaks, notification timing, 600+ experiments), Finch (gentle streaks, widgets, appointment mechanics), Calm/Headspace (daily content tab, streak limits, D30 reality), Co-Star and The Pattern (astrology-native daily push), Oura/Whoop (the "one number today" pattern and its framing), Strava (weekly/annual recap, now paywalled), Spotify Wrapped (recap loop), plus cross-cutting evidence on push benchmarks and frequency ceilings, lapsed-user win-back sequencing, PWA install rates and the iOS add-to-home-screen constraint, free-trial length vs. conversion, and the honesty/Barnum problem specific to horoscope products. India-specific layers added: WhatsApp as a delivery channel, vernacular language trust data, the existing panchang/Rahu Kaal daily-checking ritual, and AstroSage's DAU as proof of daily-habit demand in this exact market. Output ends with a buildable specification of VedicHour's daily loop, streak mechanic, weekly recap, notification copy (English/Hindi/Hinglish) and PWA install moment. NOT covered: quiz/onboarding design, pricing/paywall mechanics, SEO, or ephemeris implementation — those sit with sibling workstreams. No VedicHour code or analytics was read; all recommendations are from external evidence, not the current codebase.
